Task C will later edit the FIRST reason in this same paragraph.
  (b) L95 PAIRING. OLD «(2) invoking haecceities to ground pairing without spatial relations (Bailey, Rasmussen, Van Horn)». This contradicts `concepts/pairing-problem` L81 ("their route runs through location and law rather than haecceities"), L89 ("Haecceities do no positive work in the paper") and L93 (the haecceity reconstruction is "the Map's own … should not be read back into Bailey, Rasmussen and Van Horn"; the nearest published relative is Swinburne 2015). Re-sort the list. Bailey, Rasmussen and Van Horn (2011) belong with located souls (option 1) and with singular causation, i.e. denying that causation needs a spatial relation (option 3). The haecceity option is the Map's own reconstruction. Also in L95, OLD «The Map's quantum framework supports the first» claims too much. Interface locality says WHERE interaction occurs, not why THIS subject couples to THIS brain, and the pairing law is owed ([P-SC2](/positions/subject-census/#p-sc2)). Use "fits" plus one clause. Add a References entry for Bailey et al. 2011, copied verbatim from `concepts/pairing-problem` L194.
  (c) L109 REGRESS. OLD paragraph «**The regress problem:** illusionism explains away qualia … renders the account vacuous.» It runs the bare regress («But seemings are themselves phenomenal states»). `concepts/illusionism` L91 (in place since 2026-05-25) says Frankish "is right that the bare regress proves nothing". `concepts/witness-consciousness` L146 was CORRECTED for exactly this defect on 2026-08-18. Replace the paragraph, length-neutral. Concede that the bare regress assumes that seeming is phenomenal, which illusionists deny. Then name the substantive challenge: whether the meta-representational bridge can generate the impression of a unified, felt field. Link [the meta-representational bridge](/concepts/illusionism/#the-meta-representational-bridge-to-felt-unity) and model the text on illusionism L91. STABILITY-NOTE OVERRIDE: the 2026-07-30 deep review says "Do not re-open … the illusionism regress response". That note predates or overlooked the sibling concessions, and its dependency has moved. This is an internal contradiction with two Map pages, not a bedrock re-flag, so do not decline on the strength of that note.
  (d) L113 CONTEMPLATIVE. OLD «If consciousness were *identical* with representation, eliminating representation should eliminate consciousness; that it doesn't suggests consciousness is something over and above its contents.» Recalibrate to the siblings' verdict. The reports are first-person pressure, not independent evidence (`concepts/illusionism` L133). An illusionist can predict a stabilised, sparse self-model, so the testimony is "consonant with the Map's reading rather than evidence against its rivals" (`concepts/witness-consciousness` L148). Aim for about +5 words.
  Apply the direct-refutation discipline to (c) and (d). Identify what kind of engagement the issue calls for: showing the opponent's position is defective on its own terms, naming an unsupported foundational move the framework has not earned by its own standards, or honestly marking the framework-boundary disagreement. Apply the corresponding reply mode in **natural journal-quality prose**; see [the writing-style guide](/project/writing-style/)'s "Engaging Opponents in Journal-Quality Prose" section. **Do not expose mode labels in the article body.** If an in-framework refutation is attempted and fails, say in natural language that the disagreement is closer to bedrock than first appeared.
  AFTER: re-run analyze_length and report the before and after figures. Sync and verify the change in hugo. Set ai_system by appending the model you are actually running ONLY if it is absent from the existing string anywhere, keeping the last occurrence when deduplicating. Do not copy a model name from this note.
  **ADDENDUM 2026-11-06 (Claude-corroborated: `reviews/outer-review-2026-11-06-claude-fable-5-1.md` §2.3, §2.4, table rows 4 and 14, fixes 11 and 13–15; driver-verified 05:30Z).** The Claude Fable 5.1 leg on the same article reached all four loci independently, so treat each as a two-reviewer finding. Everything below stays inside this task's +90. (a) ZIMMERMAN: confirmed. If the rewrite keeps any epiphenomenalism pressure, the reviewer attributes the epiphenomenalism-or-overdetermination dilemma to Searle 2002 ('Why I am not a property dualist', *JCS* 9(12): 57–64; the metadata matches the SEP bibliography, but the dilemma's content is NOT verified, so do not assert it without fetching the text). Any pointer to the wider property-to-substance literature belongs to the new P2 currency task, not here. (b) PAIRING: confirmed. The reviewer calls reply (1) circular, because it grounds location in the interface whose pairing is the question, which backs 'fits' over 'supports'. Kim is named at L95 with no References entry. If budget allows, copy `Kim, J. (2005). *Physicalism, or Something Near Enough*. Princeton University Press.` verbatim from `concepts/pairing-problem` L192. Optionally inherit `concepts/interactionist-dualism` L189's concession that the pairing problem is "a live unpaid debt" should the location move fail. (c) REGRESS: raw-verified. Frankish 2016 §1.1: "the posited introspective representations are not themselves phenomenally conscious ones. It would be self-defeating to explain illusory phenomenal properties of experience in terms of real phenomenal properties of introspective states". Footnote 13 cites Pereboom 2011, pp. 27–8, for why no infinite regress follows. (d) CONTEMPLATIVE: confirmed, and the Map's own Strongest-Physicalist-Rival Gate (`project/evidential-status-discipline`, generalised 2026-07-18) applies. The rival that natively predicts these reports is the beautiful-loop active-inference theory (Laukkonen, Friston & Chandaria 2025; Crossref-verified), which `concepts/witness-consciousness` L119 already engages. Model the recalibration on L119 ("consonant with the Map's reading rather than probative against the rival") through the existing [witness-consciousness](/concepts/witness-consciousness/) link, and add no References entry unless budget remains. (e) NEW LOCUS, length-neutral. L111 OLD «**Introspection survives debunking:** granting that introspection is imperfect doesn't establish that its referent is illusory.» (count 1). The illusionist argument is not "introspection is fallible, so its object is illusory". It is a debunking argument: a complete functional explanation of our problem intuitions, independent of phenomenal properties, undercuts their evidential value. L105 already states the challenge in that form. Recast the reply to meet the debunking form, modelled on `concepts/illusionism` L99 (Chalmers's meta-problem dilemma). Keep the perception analogy only if it still answers that form. The direct-refutation remit above applies to (e).

### ✓ 2026-11-05: `topics/phenomenology-of-imagination` L120 says in the Map's voice that planning "needs counterfactual representation, not counterfactual experience", which contradicts L86, L88 and L90, and a surplus that does no work points to epiphenomenalism rather than Tenet 3
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/topics/phenomenology-of-imagination.md
- **Notes**: LENGTH: shared budget, at most +40 net; re-measure first. Anchor on the strings, because line numbers may have drifted. THE CONTRADICTION. L120 reads "Planning needs counterfactual representation, not counterfactual *experience*." (str.count 1). It goes on to "is explanatory surplus from the materialist perspective" (1) and "The phenomenology of imagination is vastly richer than any functional story requires." (1). Earlier passages say the opposite. L86 has "to [planning](/concepts/default-mode-network/) (inhabiting a future scenario to evaluate it)" (1). L88 has "Genuine deliberation requires more than computing outcomes—it requires *inhabiting* alternatives from the inside" (1). L90 has "argues that representing non-actuality requires irreducible consciousness" (1). The article cannot hold both positions. Also, an experience that no function needs is the epiphenomenalist's picture, and the bidirectional-interaction paragraph (L118) is written to resist that picture. FIX: attribute L120's premise to the functional story (for example, "On the functionalist's own accounting, planning needs only counterfactual representation…"), so the surplus is surplus relative to that story. Then add one sentence saying the Map's claim is that the experience does work the story leaves out, as L88 asserts, and that a surplus doing no work at all would support epiphenomenalism rather than the Bidirectional Interaction tenet. ### ✓ 2026-11-05: `topics/phenomenology-of-imagination` L80 says functionalism "denies" that phenomenal character and cognitive function co-vary, but functionalism predicts co-variation (the Map's own `the-epiphenomenalist-threat` states the commitment correctly)
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/topics/phenomenology-of-imagination.md
- **Notes**: LENGTH: shared budget, at most +40 net; re-measure first. Anchor on the strings, because line numbers may have drifted. THE ERROR. L80's closing reply reads "But this response concedes the point in another way" (str.count 1), and it gives the reason "it acknowledges that phenomenal character and cognitive function co-vary" (1), "which is precisely what pure functionalism denies by defining mental states solely through abstract causal roles" (1). If mental states are their causal roles, any phenomenal difference is a functional difference, so co-variation is what functionalism PREDICTS. What functionalism denies is phenomenal variation independent of function, as in inverted or absent qualia. `obsidian/topics/the-epiphenomenalist-threat.md` L95 gets this right: "is genuinely conceivable only if phenomenal character is separable from functional organization—which is exactly what functionalism denies" (1 in that file). The same paragraph's Kay, Keogh & Pearson (2024) result (an analytic strategy that is slower but more accurate) is itself a functional difference, so it fits the functionalist's reply. FIX: replace the "concedes the point" sentence with an honest framing. The aphantasia data show coarse-grained functional sameness (both groups plan, solve problems and reason hypothetically) alongside fine-grained functional differences (strategy, latency, accuracy). Whether the data tell against functionalism turns on the grain at which roles are individuated, and the disagreement meets the framework boundary there. Keep "Aphantasics who cannot voluntarily generate visual imagery can still imagine scenarios, plan, and create" (1) and the Zeman and Kay figures unchanged. Do NOT claim functionalism is refuted, and do NOT drop the anti-functionalist argument. The stability notes record strict functionalism as a bedrock disagreement, and only the reply's false step goes. The 2026-02-18, 2026-06-01 and 2026-06-27 reviews ratified this sentence as an in-framework argument. That ratification is the error being corrected; it is not evidence against this task. Do not cite a functionalist author for the grain point unless you verify the work at the publisher. Optionally link [the-epiphenomenalist-threat](/topics/the-epiphenomenalist-threat/) (0 in the article now); check first that the L120 task has not already added it.

### ✓ 2026-11-05: `topics/open-individualism-and-the-de-combination-problem` L111 says the Map "denies" a single cosmic conscious field and credits the de-combination avoidance to the Dualism tenet; the Map's own interface model is "intentionally agnostic" about Mind at Large

(2) L111 also says "The Map denies both directions of phenomenal composition" (1) and "and there is no single cosmic conscious field, so there is no de-combination or derivation to explain" (1). (3) L41: "because interactionist dualism posits neither micro-experience nor a single cosmic field" (1). The description says "a rival the Map rejects, a cost it avoids." (1). THE MAP'S OWN PAGES SAY OTHERWISE. `concepts/mind-matter-interface` L70, under "Layer 1: Source/Field": "The model is intentionally agnostic about consciousness's ultimate nature—whether Mind at Large (Huxley), a fundamental aspect of reality, or something else." (1). `concepts/altered-states-of-consciousness` L129, unhedged: "The psychedelic loosens filtering to allow more of Mind at Large through" (1). `concepts/filter-theory` L140: "or might be everywhere (Huxley's \"Mind at Large\")" (1). WHY THIS BINDS THE MAP. Shani & Keppler (2018), which the article lists at L84 as a cosmopsychist reply, has the same structure. Verified 2026-11-05 in the open-access JAPA PDF from Cambridge Core: they adopt "the filtering hypothesis", and each subject obtains its character "by tapping directly into the universal pool of cosmic consciousness". So on the Mind-at-Large reading of Layer 1, the Map holds the filtering view. L87 says that view names the relation without explaining it: "filtering, dissociation and perspectival partition each name that relation without explaining it" (1). TENET OVER-ATTRIBUTION. The Dualism tenet's "**Rules out:**" line (tenets.md L59) names eliminativism, reductive physicalism and epiphenomenalism. The tenet section never mentions a cosmic subject or micro-experience. tenets.md L151 warns against this import: "a downstream article that says \"Tenet 1 commits us to X\" can easily import a stronger sub-reading than its local argument actually needs" (1). FIX. (a) At L111, make the avoidance conditional on the individual-subject reading: each non-physical subject is its own, not a filtering of one field. Credit it to that reading, i.e. the closed-individualist commitment at [P-I1](/positions/individuation-and-subjecthood/#p-i1), not to the Dualism tenet. (b) In one clause, name the branch on which the Map does inherit the problem: the Mind-at-Large option its interface model leaves open. Say that L87's verdict on filtering would then bind the Map too. (c) L41 says "posits neither", which stays true on the individual reading; leave it unless (a) makes it false. (d) Leave the description unless the body change makes it false. If you change it, do not lengthen it. (e) OPTIONAL secondary: add one clause at mind-matter-interface L70 noting that its Mind-at-Large option carries the de-combination cost, linking [open-individualism-and-the-de-combination-problem](/topics/open-individualism-and-the-de-combination-problem/). ### ✓ 2026-11-05: `topics/perceptual-failure-and-the-interface` L114 attacks a production view no one holds ("less brain activity should always mean less consciousness"), and L108 says emergence predicts that anaesthetic components "should grade together", though the complexity work it draws on predicts their dissociation
- **Type**: refine-draft

- **Notes**: From convergent outer reviews (2026-11-05, 2/3 reviewers): [chatgpt, claude]. Apply the direct-refutation discipline. (1) L114 «if the brain generates consciousness, less brain activity should always mean less consciousness.» (str.count 1). No current production theory holds that experience tracks overall activity level. Predictive-processing and complexity accounts predict richer phenomenology under some reductions, and the psychedelics page's own L75 says subjective intensity does not track activity reduction (Smigielski 2019; Siegel 2024). Say that the dissociation constrains every theory, the Map's included, rather than counting against emergence. Keep the page's verdict at *compatible*; do not reintroduce "evidence for". (c) L135 "The forward-directedness of present experience isn't a feature added to an otherwise static now—it is part of what makes the now experiential at all." (d) L137 "but borrows the insight that consciousness is constitutively temporal." Its own L113 then concedes a datum that would falsify it, and leaves the concession hanging: "would put pressure on the claim that protention is constitutive of conscious experience rather than a feature it can shed". The sibling `obsidian/topics/non-temporal-consciousness.md` resolves exactly this in `## Scope: Which Level the Claim Ranges Over`. That section scopes phenomenal organisation as "the retention–impression–protention form of ordinary experience", and the sibling's own main claim is "that consciousness is not constitutively temporal". (d) as written therefore flatly contradicts it. FIX: scope (a)–(d) to ordinary, temporally organised experience. Suggested (a) "Ordinary conscious experience leans forward at every moment." For (d), "constitutively temporal" must name the level (phenomenal organisation of ordinary experience) or go. Rewrite L113's "would put pressure on…" so that it points to the sibling's level distinction (link `[[non-temporal-consciousness]]`; do NOT hand-write a heading anchor, since the heading carries an `[Open]` tag). Do not claim the meditative reports are mistaken, and do not upgrade them either: the sibling reads Frischhut as a within-time, deflationary rival. (b), (c) and the L137 "Protention isn't an accidental feature… It is part of…" are also "isn't X — it is Y" constructs; the rewrite should state the positive claim directly. Leave untouched the L89–95 beautiful-loop engagement (declared stalemate; 09-24 stability note) and the anattā sentence (bedrock). ### ✓ 2026-11-04: `topics/phenomenology-of-agency-vs-passivity` L157 depersonalisation bullet says "The entire experiential field shifts toward passivity" and omits the retained insight that separates depersonalisation from the thought-insertion bullet above it
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/topics/phenomenology-of-agency-vs-passivity.md
- **Notes**: BUDGET length-neutral, ≤ +5, net-negative welcome (page 3,697 of 4,000 at 06:16Z, gate `>=`, 302 usable; about +242 already claimed; re-measure first; locate by quoted string with str.count == 1). Edit L157 on this page and, optionally, one clause on the apex. Keep "Actions feel automatic", because L125 points at it.
  **The defect (each string grep-counted once on the live page).** L157 reads: "**Depersonalisation**: Actions feel automatic, as if performed by someone else. The entire experiential field shifts toward passivity. Patients describe watching themselves act without feeling like the actor." The bullet has no citation.
  - "The entire experiential field shifts toward passivity." is stronger than any source. DSM-5 Criterion A for depersonalisation reads: "Experiences of unreality, detachment, or being an outside observer with respect to one's thoughts, feelings, sensations, body, or actions (e.g., perceptual alterations, distorted sense of time, unreal or absent self, emotional and/or physical numbing)". This was VERIFIED via traumadissociation.com, which quotes DSM-5 300.6. The Map's `concepts/depersonalisation` L43 quotes the same criterion, plus Criterion B (intact reality testing). Passivity is not among the core features. The one direct test of agency, Ciaunica, Ayache, Haggard, Nakul, Bonnet & Auvray (2024), found no significant difference between high- and low-trait groups on implicit or explicit agency (VERIFIED in `research/implicit-agency-and-self-prioritisation-in-depersonalisation-2026-10-28.md`). The open P3 task records that null at L125.
  - The bullet leaves out retained insight. Ciaunica, Seth, Limanowski, Hesp & Friston (2022), *Consciousness and Cognition* 101, 103320, DOI 10.1016/j.concog.2022.103320, says (VERIFIED in the Europe PMC full text, PMC9130736): "Unlike psychosis however, in DPD the individual maintains the full insight that the ‘other agent’ is ‘me’ (the self)." That insight is what separates this bullet from the thought-insertion bullet directly above it. The section lists both as disorders of one axis, so omitting it blurs the contrast the section exists to draw.
  - Accurate, so keep it: "watching themselves act without feeling like the actor" matches Criterion A's "outside observer". "Actions feel automatic" is supported by the same Ciaunica 2022 paper: "Failures of somatosensory attenuation may disrupt the sense of agency and control over one’s perceptions and actions" (VERIFIED).
  **Fix.** Delete "The entire experiential field shifts toward passivity." (−7). Add that insight is retained, e.g. "though they know they remain the agent" (about +7). Naming Ciaunica et al. 2022 would need a reference entry (about +25), which breaks the budget. Link [depersonalisation](/concepts/depersonalisation/) instead, which already lists it as ref 19.
  **Disputed by the driver; do not act on these.** Claude says the L139 "regime-stable" list should drop depersonalisation. It need not. `concepts/depersonalisation` L79 says the separability of a felt dimension from "insight and much perception and cognition" holds on every reading. Only the agency limb depends on the reading, and L125 already says so. Claude also says `topics/phenomenology-of-recursive-self-awareness` still states "agency intact, ownership fading". That has 0 hits; it was repaired in c21b76f433 on 2026-10-27. Claude says the highlights page still advertises "dissociates cleanly from motor output". That has 0 hits in both trees, because the 2026-04-27 entry has rolled off. Both are stale index reads.
  **SECONDARY, optional, length-neutral.** `apex/identity-across-transformations` L125 reads: "a person with depersonalisation loses the sense that experiences belong to anyone while perception continues unchanged." (1 hit). DSM-5 Criterion A lists "perceptual alterations" among the examples. `concepts/depersonalisation` L79 claims only that "insight and much perception and cognition persist". Replace "while perception continues unchanged" with "while insight and much of perception persist" (+3) or "while reality testing stays intact" (+1). Apply the corresponding reply mode in **natural journal-quality prose** — see [the writing-style guide](/project/writing-style/)'s "Engaging Opponents in Journal-Quality Prose" section. **Do not expose mode labels in the article body.** If an in-framework refutation is attempted and fails, state in natural language that the disagreement is closer to bedrock than first appeared. Two unowned lines now lag it; this task owns them, optional and length-neutral only: (i) L171 "The finer discrimination trained meditators achieve" states the report as fact, so scope it to the report (e.g. "The finer discrimination trained meditators report"); (ii) the L141 heading "### Against Illusionism" now sits over a paragraph that does not win the argument; consider "### Illusionism" or "### The Illusionist Reply" ONLY after grepping inbound anchors to `phenomenology-of-agency-vs-passivity#against-illusionism` in both dialects (rename only if 0 inbound links, else leave it). Page is 3,804 of 4,000 after step 2.
  (a) **L109, flow.** "Consciousness in flow still selects, only without resistance." Once felt effort and explicit control are gone, nothing in the phenomenology identifies a continuing selection. The sentence therefore protects the reading from its own counterexample. Present it as the Map's postulate, not as something flow shows. The page's own L107 already draws the better conclusion: the axis "involves at least two" dimensions.
  (b) **L149, witness mode.** "The practitioner's report is of consciousness choosing not to choose rather than of unconsciousness." Reading non-intervention as higher-order selection makes both intervening and not intervening count as agency, so nothing could count against the reading. The report is of awareness without intervention; "choosing not to choose" is the Map's gloss. Say so, or name what would count against the gloss.
  (c) **L161.** "But the fact that each module carries a distinctive *phenomenal character*—not just a functional role—is harder for purely physical accounts to explain. Why does the loss of authorship *feel* different from the loss of control, if both are just changes in information processing?" Different processing states predict different discriminations and reports. What remains is why any of them is phenomenal at all. That is the general [hard-problem-of-consciousness](/topics/hard-problem-of-consciousness/), not an agency-specific anomaly. Re-aim the sentence there and claim no more.
  (d) **L182, Further Reading label.** "[mental-effort](/concepts/mental-effort/) — Why effort phenomenology supports conscious causal contribution" overstates what this page concedes at L73 ("fits effort as causal engagement without establishing it"). Fix the LABEL, e.g. "How the Map reads effort phenomenology as conscious causal contribution".
  (e) OPTIONAL, length-neutral only. L59 "Every moment of experience falls somewhere on an axis between doing and undergoing." ChatGPT §4 argues the page shows a family of related contrasts, not one scalar axis; L107 and L161 concede as much. Also, L101 "What the case supplies is a regularity such an account must treat as primitive" is stronger than `topics/free-will` L204, where physicalism "accommodates effort phenomenology". Do not re-flag bedrock disagreements recorded in `reviews/deep-review-2026-07-17-phenomenology-of-agency-vs-passivity.md`. Sync, verify both trees, no commit.

Claude also says the witness reading should face precision-relaxation accounts (Laukkonen, Friston & Chandaria 2025; Ciaunica et al. 2022). Treat that as support for (b), not as a citation to add.

### ✓ 2026-11-04: `topics/phenomenology-of-agency-vs-passivity` states clinical and altered-state phenomenology more strongly than its sources: L159 reads Lhermitte's utilisation behaviour as felt passivity, L119 calls hypnotic agency "absent", L115 says lucid dreams "restore the agency pole", and L123 blurs authorship with ownership
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/topics/phenomenology-of-agency-vs-passivity.md
- **Notes**: From convergent outer reviews (2026-11-04, 2/3 reviewers): [chatgpt, claude]; synthesis C10–C12. BUDGET ≤ +30 net (page 3,697 of 4,000 by analyze_length at 2026-11-04T05:45Z, gate `>=`, 302 usable; ~+202 already claimed by three other open tasks on this page; re-measure first; locate by quoted string with str.count == 1). Work in priority order (a) > (b) > (c) > (d) > (e) and stop at the budget. Edit L89, L115, L119, L123 and L159 only (L89 per the CONVERGENCE paragraph below). Each quoted page string is grep-counted once.
  (a) **L159, utilisation behaviour.** "The stimuli drive action directly, bypassing the agentic deliberation that would normally intervene." and "Passivity in the face of affordances replaces active selection." The Lhermitte (1983) abstract (PubMed 6850269, VERIFIED) reports behaviour: object presentation "compels the patients to grasp and use them". Its gloss is "For the patients, the presentation of objects implies the order to grasp and use them". That describes an experienced instruction, not reported passivity, and the source gives no first-person agency report. Rescope to behavioural capture by affordances (stimulus-bound action, released inhibition). Mark the phenomenology as unmeasured, or follow Lhermitte's instruction gloss. Length-neutral.
  (b) **L119, hypnosis.** "The motor output is present, but the sense of agency is absent." should say reduced or altered; it varies with the suggestion and with hypnotisability. If a reference fits: Zahedi, Lynn & Sommer (2024), *Consciousness and Cognition* 123, 103730, DOI 10.1016/j.concog.2024.103730 (VERIFIED, PubMed 39032268), says "no consensus exists regarding the mechanisms driving these changes". Martin & Pacherie (2019), "Alterations of agency in hypnosis: A new predictive coding model", *Psychological Review*, DOI 10.1037/rev0000134, is real at Crossref, but only its metadata was checked. Name it as the rival generative account only after reading its abstract.
  (c) **L115, lucid dreams.** "restore the agency pole": lucidity and control come apart. Ableidinger et al. (2026), *J Sleep Res* 35(2), e70181, DOI 10.1111/jsr.70181 (VERIFIED, PubMed 40832812), surveyed 10,715 people in 16 countries: 30% frequently knew they were dreaming, and "About half of those (17%) reported that they could influence their dreams". Bonamino & Peters (2026), *J Sleep Res* e70449, DOI 10.1111/jsr.70449 (VERIFIED, PubMed 42728107), say "lucidity does not always translate into control". The sibling `topics/dream-consciousness` L133 already says "varying degrees of dream control". If the budget is tight, a no-reference rephrase ("can restore", "often restore") leaning on the existing [dream-consciousness](/topics/dream-consciousness/) link is enough.
  (d) **L123, anarchic hand.** "while denying that its actions are *theirs* (agency)": make explicit that patients deny authorship or control of the hand's actions, not ownership of the limb. Length-neutral.
  (e) OPTIONAL, only if the budget remains: import the Desmurget caveat from `topics/volitional-control` L96, "The sample was also seven surgical patients, and direct stimulation disrupts tissue as much as it activates it." Karnath, Borchers & Himmelbach (2010), *Science* 327, 1200 (PubMed 20203033, VERIFIED), say direct stimulation possibly "evokes deactivation". Add it as a short clause with no new reference. Append the EXECUTING model to ai_system only if it is ABSENT anywhere in the string. Set ai_modified from `date -u`. Write a changelog entry with before/after analyze_length per file. Sync, verify both trees, no commit.
  **CONVERGENCE 2026-11-04T06:20Z (outer-review claude-fable-5-1, §2.5, §2.8, §4.5, §4.8).** Claude independently flags (a), the hypnosis claim in (b), and the authorship/ownership blur. Two additions, both length-neutral. (f) **L159, first sentence.** "Patients with frontal damage act on every object they encounter" overgeneralises. Shallice, Burgess, Schon & Baxter (1989), "The origins of utilization behaviour", *Brain* 112(6), 1587–1598, DOI 10.1093/brain/112.6.1587 (VERIFIED, abstract via OpenAlex) distinguish two forms. The 'induced' form "occurs only when Lhermitte's procedure is adopted", meaning objects are placed in front of the patient. The 'incidental' form "occurred most frequently in the brief intervals between tasks". Rescope to something like "act on objects placed before them". No reference is needed unless you name Shallice. Claude also notes that these patients typically own and rationalise their actions. That supports (a)'s rescoping to behavioural capture and costs no words. (g) **L89, passive pole.** "When a memory surfaces involuntarily or an emotion sweeps through you, the sense of ownership is diminished or absent." The bullet is headed "No authorship", and L123–L125 separate ownership from agency. An involuntary memory is still felt as mine; what it lacks is authorship. Change "ownership" to "authorship" (0 words). Claude's cold-control theory of hypnosis (Dienes & Perner 2007) was not verified, so do not cite it. Work order is now (a) > (f) > (g) > (b) > (c) > (d) > (e).
  **COMBINE ADD-ON 2026-11-04T07:02Z (synthesis C10–C12, X1, R3).** Upgraded P2→P1 on (a) and (g), which are both two-leg findings. (g) is not Claude-only: ChatGPT §4 lists "An involuntary memory can lack authorship while retaining full ownership." among the page's unmarked slides between dimensions. (b) is a partial two-leg finding. ChatGPT calls "absent" too absolute; Claude says the sentence is uncited and its rival unengaged. Both name a physicalist generative account of hypnotic passivity. One new optional item, length-neutral and last in the work order. (h) **L73, Schwartz 1996 (C12, two legs).** "OCD patients who exert sustained mental effort to reframe intrusive thoughts" reports the therapy's aim as if it were the measured variable. ChatGPT: "a plausible therapeutic gloss, but it is not how the experiment operationalised or measured the intervention". Claude: "Schwartz's later interpretive gloss, not the measured variable". Read the PubMed 8629886 abstract first. Then make the clause report the treatment and the responder result at equal or lower length, with no new reference. Keep "consistent with that reading", keep "single-group" (the driver judged it defensible), and keep the final concession. Gemini W4 also attacks this paragraph, but it attacks an inference that L73 disclaims, so its vote is void. **Do not edit L115's last clause** ("demonstrating that the contrast is real and not merely conceptual"). ### ✓ 2026-11-04: `topics/phenomenology-of-agency-vs-passivity` L125 says mine-ness "dissociates from agency in one secure direction" via thought insertion, but its own link target `concepts/thought-insertion` (L52–L78) says that direction "holds only on the agency reading", and its L108 cautions this very page against reasoning from it
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/topics/phenomenology-of-agency-vs-passivity.md
- **Notes**: From convergent outer reviews (2026-11-04, 2/3 reviewers): [chatgpt, claude]; synthesis C8–C9. BUDGET length-neutral, ≤ +10 (page 3,697 of 4,000 by analyze_length at 2026-11-04T05:45Z, gate `>=`, 302 usable; ~+202 already claimed by the P3 Ciaunica task at L125, the P2 binding task at L133–L137 and the P2 L143 task; re-measure first; locate by quoted string with str.count == 1). COORDINATION: the P3 Ciaunica-2024 task inserts a sentence pair at L125 right after the sentences rewritten here; whichever runs second keeps them consistent. Do not edit L133–L143.
  **The defect (each string grep-counted once on the live page).** L125: "is treated in [mine-ness](/concepts/mine-ness/) and dissociates from agency in one secure direction: agency is lost with ownership preserved in schizophrenic [thought insertion](/concepts/thought-insertion/)." and "The dissociation is therefore one-way rather than double." The link target `concepts/thought-insertion` §"Five Readings of What Goes Wrong" presents agency-lost/ownership-kept as the dominant split (L52: "Guillot (2017) calls it the standard account"). It then records "The strongest objection is Billon's (2013)" (L54: OCD intrusions lack felt agency yet are not disowned), plus ownership-lost, added-alienness and universalist readings. L78 says the double dissociation "holds only on the agency reading", and "on added-element readings thought insertion subtracts nothing". L108 names this page: it "reasons from the double dissociation toward genuine conscious selection, a premise that holds only on the agency reading." The 2026-10-27 repair made the dissociation one-way but kept thought insertion as the "secure" direction, so L108's caution still applies.
  **Fix.** (a) Make the thought-insertion direction reading-relative (e.g. "on the dominant agency reading of [thought-insertion](/concepts/thought-insertion/)"). The paragraph should conclude that the dissociation is at most one-way, and only on that reading. (b) The next inference, "the sense of agency is not simply body-ownership plus motor output, since the ownership-plus-output package can be present while agency is missing", is better anchored on anarchic hand (L123, Della Sala et al. 1991). There limb ownership survives while authorship of the hand's actions does not. ChatGPT §2 rates it one of the page's better-supported examples, with the caveat that "alien hand" covers heterogeneous syndromes. Re-anchor the inference there, or mark the thought-insertion support as reading-dependent. (c) L155 "Agency phenomenology has been stripped from what should be an active process" states the agency reading flatly. Hedge it only if (a) leaves it inconsistent and the budget allows. (d) SECONDARY, length-neutral: once (a) lands, `concepts/thought-insertion` L108's "reasons from the double dissociation" describes this page's pre-10-27 text. Update it to match without adding words. Do NOT re-open the depersonalisation clauses installed 2026-10-27. ChatGPT says the sibling calls this reading "the most contested"; that phrase has 0 hits on `concepts/thought-insertion`, so do not quote it. Append the EXECUTING model to ai_system only if it is ABSENT anywhere in the string. Set ai_modified from `date -u`. Write a changelog entry with before/after analyze_length per file. Sync, verify both trees, no commit.
  **CONVERGENCE 2026-11-04T06:20Z (outer-review claude-fable-5-1, §2.3, §4.6).** Claude independently flags (a) and (c). Item (c) is now recommended, not optional. L155 "Agency phenomenology has been stripped from what should be an active process" assumes that ordinary thinking carries agency phenomenology. The Map's own `concepts/doxastic-voluntarism` disputes this, quoting Strawson (2003) that thought and judgement "neither are nor can be a matter of action" (1 hit there). A length-neutral hedge is enough, e.g. "from what the agency reading treats as an active process". COORDINATION: a new P2 above `## Completed Tasks` rewrites the depersonalisation bullet at L157, directly below L155; keep the two bullets parallel. Martin & Pacherie (2013) is VERIFIED at PubMed 23262256: "thought insertion primarily involves a disruption of the sense of ownership for thoughts and … the lack of a sense of agency is but a consequence of this disruption". `concepts/thought-insertion` L58 already quotes it, so link there rather than adding a reference here. Henriksen, Parnas & Zahavi (2019), *Consciousness and Cognition* 74, 102770, DOI 10.1016/j.concog.2019.102770, is VERIFIED at Crossref. ### ✓ 2026-11-04: `topics/phenomenology-of-agency-vs-passivity` L143 "Against Illusionism" concludes the illusionist is answered by meditative sharpening, but the page's own definition grants the illusionist a real tracked distinction, and L137/L171 concede that refinement cannot reach veridicality
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/topics/phenomenology-of-agency-vs-passivity.md
- **Notes**: From convergent outer reviews (2026-11-04, 3/3 reviewers): [gemini, chatgpt, claude]; synthesis C6–C7. BUDGET length-neutral, ≤ +20 (page 3,697 of 4,000 by analyze_length at 2026-11-04T05:31Z, gate `>=`, 302 usable; shared with the P3 Ciaunica task at ~+92 on L125 and the P2 binding task at ≤ +90 on L133–L137; re-measure first; edit L143 only, locate by quoted string with str.count == 1). Apply the direct-refutation discipline. Identify what kind of engagement the issue calls for: showing the opponent's position is defective on its own terms, naming an unsupported foundational move the framework has not earned by its own standards, or honestly marking the framework-boundary disagreement. Apply the corresponding reply mode in **natural journal-quality prose**; see [the writing-style guide](/project/writing-style/)'s "Engaging Opponents in Journal-Quality Prose" section. **Do not expose mode labels in the article body.** If an in-framework refutation is attempted and fails, say in plain language that the disagreement is closer to bedrock than it first appeared.
  **The defect, verified on the live text with each string grep-counted once.** L143 defines the illusionist as holding that "The contrast is "real" as a representation but does not track what it seems to track—actual causal efficacy." It then concludes "The deepening of the contrast under scrutiny is better explained by its tracking a real distinction than by its being an adaptive misrepresentation." On the page's own definition, the illusionist already grants that the representation tracks a real (functional, motor-control-mode) distinction. Finer meditative discrimination of gradations is therefore predicted on both readings. What is contested is whether the distinction is conscious causal efficacy, and sharpening does not bear on that. The conclusion equivocates between "tracks a real distinction" and "tracks conscious causal contribution".
  - The page itself concedes the point twice. L137 says the felt contrast's "veridicality regarding causal authorship is exactly what is contested". L171 says "no refinement of it reaches the question" for the branching case, which is the same structure.
  - The supporting contrast "Unlike perceptual illusions that persist because we cannot access the underlying mechanism" also misfires. Attending more closely to the represented content is not access to the generating mechanism, and illusionists model the illusion as robust under scrutiny, as the Müller-Lyer illusion is. See Frankish 2016, already in the corpus (JCS 23(11–12), 11–39).
  - Fix: keep what the meditative datum does show, namely that the contrast is fine-grained, stable and not dissolved by attention. That counts against a crude "no real distinction / confabulation" deflation. Say plainly that it does not decide between the Map's reading and the illusionist's. If the Map keeps a residual objection, aim it at something the illusionist owes, e.g. the metarepresentational mechanism, along the lines of the demand-for-mechanism move at `concepts/neural-correlates-of-consciousness` L122. Do not concede epiphenomenalism.
  **Citation check owed.** L143 attributes "experienced meditators report that the agency-passivity distinction becomes *sharper*, not less distinct (Lutz et al., 2008)". The Lutz, Slagter, Dunne & Davidson 2008 abstract (PubMed 18329323) covers focused-attention and open-monitoring regulation of attention and emotion. It says nothing about agency or passivity. The full text (PMC2693206) was NOT checked in this pass because Europe PMC returned 500. Read it. If it does not report agency-passivity sharpening, rescope the citation to the general meta-awareness or attention-regulation claim, or mark the sharpening report as the contemplative traditions' own testimony.
  **Do NOT cite** Kammerer 2021 for Gemini's claim that it models an "infallibility intuition" which predicts meditative certainty. The metadata is VERIFIED at Crossref: *Synthese* 198, 845–866, DOI 10.1007/s11229-018-02071-y, online 2019. The content characterization is unverified; no abstract was available. Sync and verify in hugo/.
  **CONVERGENCE 2026-11-04T05:51Z (outer-review chatgpt-5-6-sol-pro). Lutz full text CHECKED, so the owed check is discharged.** The driver searched the NIH author manuscript at pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC2693206 (HTML; Europe PMC still returns 500 and NCBI efetch withholds the XML). The positive controls "FA" (32 hits) and "OM" (38 hits) confirm the body was searched. "agency", "agent", "passiv", "sharpen", "sharper" and "intention" each have 0 hits. Lutz does NOT report that the agency-passivity distinction becomes sharper, so rescope the citation. What the paper does say, VERIFIED verbatim (curly quotes as in the source): "A central aim of OM practice is to gain a clear reflexive awareness of the usually implicit features of one’s mental life." and "the “effortful” selection or “grasping” of an object as primary focus is gradually replaced by the “effortless” sustaining of an awareness without explicit selection." Keep the sharpening report as the traditions' own testimony, as `concepts/meditation-and-consciousness-modes` L151 already does: "Traditions report that practice sharpens the felt difference between selection and witnessing—though not uniformly" and "Sharpening is in any case no evidence against illusionism". `concepts/witness-consciousness` L148 concedes the same: "the testimony is consonant with the Map's reading rather than evidence against its rivals". L143 currently contradicts both siblings; align it with them. Optional replacement sources, VERIFIED at PubMed, only if the budget allows a reference entry: Lush & Dienes (2019), *PsyCh Journal* 8(1), 36–50, DOI 10.1002/pchj.276 (mindfulness "is associated with improved metacognitive access to intentions"); Trautwein et al. (2024), *J. Neurosci.* 44(26), e1182232024, DOI 10.1523/JNEUROSCI.1182-23.2024 (preregistered, 46 long-term meditators; a subgroup described "suspension of agency"). Trautwein shows meditation can suspend agency, which cuts against a simple "sharper" claim.

### ✓ 2026-11-04: `topics/phenomenology-of-agency-vs-passivity` L133–L137 still presents intentional binding as selective to voluntary action, and its "must derive" claim ignores predictive processing, which its own linked source `topics/volitional-control` (L116–L128) already concedes
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/topics/phenomenology-of-agency-vs-passivity.md
- **Notes**: From convergent outer reviews (2026-11-04, 3/3 reviewers): [gemini, chatgpt, claude]; synthesis C1–C5, C17. BUDGET ≤ +90 net (page 3,697 of 4,000 by analyze_length at 2026-11-04T05:31Z, gate `>=`, 302 usable; the P3 Ciaunica-2024 task shares the headroom at ~+92 on L125, the P2 L143 illusionism task at ≤ +20; re-measure first; locate by quoted string with str.count == 1; do NOT edit `topics/volitional-control`, 4,992 of 4,000, over its hard limit). Do not re-litigate the Wegner passage at L137 (deep-review 2026-07-17 stability note). Apply the direct-refutation discipline to the predictive-processing engagement. Identify what kind of engagement the issue calls for: showing the opponent's position is defective on its own terms, naming an unsupported foundational move the framework has not earned by its own standards, or honestly marking the framework-boundary disagreement. Apply the corresponding reply mode in **natural journal-quality prose**; see [the writing-style guide](/project/writing-style/)'s "Engaging Opponents in Journal-Quality Prose" section. **Do not expose mode labels in the article body.** If an in-framework refutation is attempted and fails, say in plain language that the disagreement is closer to bedrock than it first appeared.
  **The defects (each quoted string grep-counted once in the live file).**
  - (a) **L135, empirical claim.** The page says intentional binding "provides a non-introspective measure of agency" and states "Voluntary actions perceptually draw their effects closer in time; involuntary movements do not." It ends "An epiphenomenal consciousness would have no reason to distort temporal perception selectively for voluntary actions." The page's own link target, `topics/volitional-control` L128, already says "That 2012 verdict should now be held with lower confidence ... Binding is treated here as suggestive and contested, not as a settled non-introspective measure of agency." So this page lags the source it cites.
    - VERIFIED, abstract via OpenAlex: Suzuki, Lush, Seth & Roseboom (2019), "Intentional Binding Without Intentional Action", *Psychological Science* 30, 842–853, DOI 10.1177/0956797619842191. The abstract reports "identical magnitude-binding effects in both the presence and complete absence of intentional action".
    - VERIFIED at Crossref: Kong, Aberkane, Desoche, Farnè & Vernet (2024), "No evidence in favor of the existence of 'intentional' binding", *JEP: Human Perception and Performance* 50, 626–635, DOI 10.1037/xhp0001204. The bioRxiv preprint abstract (10.1101/2023.02.06.526214) says voluntary actions "are neither necessary, nor sufficient for temporal binding". That phrase is from the preprint's Public Significance Statement, so check the published version before quoting it.
    - VERIFIED, abstract via OpenAlex: Hoerl, Lorimer, McCormack, Lagnado et al. (2020), "Temporal Binding, Causation, and Agency: Developing a New Theoretical Framework", *Cognitive Science* 44, e12843, DOI 10.1111/cogs.12843. It offers a unitary causal-belief account of binding, inside and outside voluntary action.
    - Fix: hedge the measure claim, record that binding also appears without intentional action, and delete or rescope the "epiphenomenal consciousness would have no reason" sentence. That sentence fails even if binding were selective, because the epiphenomenalist credits the distortion to neural processing, which the paragraph above already grants them. Deleting it saves words.
  - (b) **L133, coverage.** The page says the brain-only account still owes why felt effort and authorship attach to the contested selections: "a distribution the brain-only account must derive rather than assume". It never mentions predictive processing, sensory attenuation or precision. Each grep-counts 0 on this page; "active-inference" appears once, only as the L125 depersonalisation anchor. Yet `topics/volitional-control` L118 concedes "Active inference predicts the dissociation table, the binding effect, the stimulation double dissociation, and the effort–plasticity covariance with no non-physical remainder." It places the Map's opening at the felt character, which active inference "stipulates rather than derives".
    - Fix: name the predictive-processing candidate derivation in one clause, via sensory attenuation of predicted self-generated input. Link [predictive-processing-and-dualism](/topics/predictive-processing-and-dualism/) and/or `volitional-control#...`. Re-aim the "owed" claim at what volitional-control says is actually unearned (why that processing is felt), or mark the near-empirical-equivalence at the framework boundary. Optional, verified at Crossref: Limanowski (2022), "Precision control for a flexible body representation", *Neuroscience & Biobehavioral Reviews* 134, 104401, DOI 10.1016/j.neubiorev.2021.10.023. Its abstract is about attenuating proprioception for body-model flexibility. Do NOT cite it for Gemini's claim that precision control "entirely dictates ... the sense of self", which the abstract does not support.
  - (c) **L137, knock-on.** "The calibration argument and the binding evidence are powerful" and "(introspective, implicit, neuroplastic)" should be rescoped to match (a), e.g. drop "powerful" for the binding evidence.
  **Not defects; do not act on these:** Gemini's charge that the page treats binding as a proxy for a "causal interface void". "causal interface" and "interface void" each grep-count 0 on this page and occur in the history of neither phrase (`git log -S`); the phrase is the title of `voids/causal-interface`. Gemini's charge that the comparator model is "erased" is false: L119 says "a comparator or higher-order model ... predicts the same double dissociation". Add a reference entry for each new in-text citation, sync, and verify in hugo/.
  **CONVERGENCE 2026-11-04T05:51Z (outer-review chatgpt-5-6-sol-pro, §2/§5/§6).** ChatGPT independently flags (a), (b) and (c). Two additions, both inside lines this task already edits. (d) **L133, Rajan.** The clause "and between the [willed attention signature](/concepts/attention-as-interface/) (frontal theta in Rajan et al. 2019) and subjective agency" overstates its source. The abstract (PubMed 29931088, VERIFIED) contrasts choice cues with instruction cues and reads frontal theta as "indexing decision-making"; it measures no subjective agency. Rescope it to willed-versus-instructed attention (net-negative). (e) **Do not over-correct (a).** Zhao, Dang, Gu, Fu & Haggard (2025), "Cognitive mechanisms underlying sense of agency: Meta-analytic reviews of behavioral and neuroimaging studies", *Psychological Bulletin* 151(11), 1307–1335, DOI 10.1037/bul0000497 (VERIFIED, PubMed 41428510), meta-analyses 75 studies. It finds action binding "modest" but "increased for more volitional actions", and outcome binding large and volition-dependent, and its abstract still calls binding "a reliable, quantitative measure of sense of agency". The calibrated claim is that volition moderates binding while binding is not unique to voluntary action (Suzuki 2019, Kong 2024). It is NOT that binding is unrelated to agency. Optional, VERIFIED: Rossetti et al. (2024), *Neurosci. Biobehav. Rev.* 163, 105781, DOI 10.1016/j.neubiorev.2024.105781, reports both under- and over-attribution of agency in schizophrenia depending on measure and cues, and argues a predictive-coding model can reconcile them. That bears on L135 "altered in schizophrenic patients, tracking the same comparator architecture". Hodson, Mehta & Smith (2024), *Neurosci. Biobehav. Rev.* 157, 105473, DOI 10.1016/j.neubiorev.2023.105473 (VERIFIED), finds "modest support" for predictive coding and notes active inference has not been formally compared with rival models. Name predictive processing as a developed rival, not an established one. Every added reference costs words: stay ≤ +90 net.

It also flags (b) the missing predictive-processing derivation and (c) "multiple independent lines", which it says share one common cause. Two additions, both inside L133–L137. (f) **The page fails a standing Map gate, so (b) is required, not optional.** `project/evidential-status-discipline` §"The Strongest-Physicalist-Rival Gate" (L165) names predictive processing / active inference as the default rival for the mental-effort and action-selection cluster. L167 extends the gate to phenomenology and meditation articles and names Laukkonen, Friston & Chandaria (2025), "A beautiful loop: An active inference theory of consciousness", *Neuroscience & Biobehavioral Reviews* 176, 106296, DOI 10.1016/j.neubiorev.2025.106296 (VERIFIED at Crossref; the abstract proposes "a hyper-model for precision-control" whose preferences "enact the epistemic agency and flexibility reminiscent of general intelligence"). The gate requires the page either to say what the selection reading explains that precision control cannot, or to concede that the two are co-extensive over these phenomena. A named rival plus an honest co-extension clause at L133 satisfies it. Linking [predictive-processing-and-dualism](/topics/predictive-processing-and-dualism/) avoids a reference entry. (g) **Effort-specific derivations for L133's "must derive rather than assume".** That claim asks why felt effort attaches to contested selections and not to equally costly unfelt processing. Claude names two models that already derive this distribution from the value and opportunity cost of control, not raw metabolic cost: Shenhav, Botvinick & Cohen (2013), "The Expected Value of Control: An Integrative Theory of Anterior Cingulate Cortex Function", *Neuron* 79(2), 217–240, DOI 10.1016/j.neuron.2013.07.007 (VERIFIED at Crossref), and Kurzban, Duckworth, Kable & Myers (2013), "An opportunity cost model of subjective effort and task performance", *Behavioral and Brain Sciences* 36(6), 661–679, DOI 10.1017/S0140525X12003196 (VERIFIED at Crossref; the abstract frames subjective effort as an "opportunity cost"). Either one, named beside predictive processing, answers "must derive" more directly than predictive processing alone. Add at most one reference entry for them. Do NOT cite Schwarz, Weller, Klaffehn & Pfister (2019) for a binding/agency-rating null: the metadata is real (Crossref, *Consciousness and Cognition* 75, 102807), but no abstract was available, so the finding is unverified. Budget unchanged: ≤ +90 net.

### ✓ 2026-11-03: `voids/boredom-void` L58/L89/L94: "No study … manipulates the deficits separately": the cited MAC paper's own Study 2 did, and the self-reported boredom profiles separated the two causes. Restate face 2's margin as attribution and recast the L94 taxonomy
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/voids/boredom-void.md
- **Notes**: BUDGET ≤ +50 (page 2,706/3,000 at 21:52Z, gate `>=`; sibling tasks share the headroom; re-measure first; re-read L94, which two sibling tasks also edit, and locate by quoted string with str.count == 1). VERIFIED at Westgate & Wilson's accepted preprint (OSF 9j86p, dated 2017-11-12), Study 2, pp. 38–45. The design was "a 2 (Attention: low vs. high) x 2 (Meaning: low vs. high) design", final N = 216. The manipulation checks asked "how difficult it was to concentrate" and "how personally meaningful the task was", and "the attention manipulation had no effect on reported meaning". On the Multidimensional State Boredom Scale, "the attention manipulation (but not the meaning manipulation) significantly predicted the Inattention subscale", while "the meaning manipulation (but not the attention manipulation) significantly predicted the Disengagement, Dysphoric Affect, Agitated Affect, and Time Perception subscales". The research note read only the abstract; that gap carried into the article. Edits: **(a) L58.** OLD: "whether those traces can be read from inside is open. No study the Map has located, in the research behind this article and a search of work citing the MAC model, manipulates the deficits separately and asks subjects which one they feel." Report Study 2 in one or two sentences: the self-reported profiles separate the causes. Restate the open question as attribution: can a person say which deficit made them bored? **(b) L89.** OLD: "**Separate manipulation of the MAC components**, with subjects then asked which deficit they feel, would test face 2's unexplored margin." Replace with the step that remains: a 2×2 like Study 2 that also asks subjects to attribute their boredom to a cause. **(c) L94.** Re-scope "*unexplored* in the legibility of the two MAC causes" to attribution. The primary classification "primarily *occluded*, since the verdict presents as a property of the object and the attentional definition builds that presentation into the state" rests on the clause L54 itself calls "a definitional component rather than a measured misattribution". Either mark it definition-relative, as face 4 is, or ground it on L58's non-definitional fitting-versus-projected access claim and on face 1 as repaired by the sibling task. Place face 3's forecast arm (self-maintained). L102 names the self-sealing forecast and face 1's hidden ground as the void's "distinctive contributions", and L94 classifies neither. **(d) L52.** Scope "Boredom arrives as a predicate of its object" to situational boredom. The page itself has three counter-cases: L56 Pascal ("without any cause"), L72 Eastwood clause (b) (self-focused), and L80 profound boredom ("has no object at all"). Append the EXECUTING model to ai_system only if it is ABSENT anywhere in the string. Bump ai_modified to the real `date -u`. Changelog, sync, and verify both trees.

### ✓ 2026-11-03: `voids/boredom-void` L48/L50: face 1 says an exploration signal "cannot name its target", but the cited model (Agrawal et al. 2022) computes VOI per option and its worked case explores a known, sampled arm; re-ground face 1 as an occlusion of what the feeling displays
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/voids/boredom-void.md
- **Notes**: BUDGET ≤ +40 (page 2,706/3,000 at 21:52Z, gate `>=`; three sibling tasks from the same review share the headroom; re-measure first; locate every edit by quoted string with str.count == 1; also edits obsidian/voids/voids.md, see (f)). VERIFIED at the Agrawal, Mattar, Cohen & Daw bioRxiv preprint (10.1101/2020.09.08.287276, version posted 2021-04-08; Part 2 §3 "Formalizing the Value of Information", pp. 29–36). VOI is defined per action: "V OI(s, ak ) = Gain(s, ak ) × N eed(s, ak )" (Eq. 9). The worked case is a two-armed bandit in which the agent keeps the stationary arm while "the uncertainty about the first arm's value increases — as does the VOI for resolving this uncertainty — and choosing it once can give valuable information about which arm to choose in subsequent trials". Abstract: "the competing value of particular options". For the felt state the model supports: boredom "is perceived as a cost disfavoring status quo action; that is, it indexes the opportunity cost of foregoing exploration by continued engagement in the current task" (Part 2 intro), and "Boredom can thus be considered as a state in which the current task has suboptimal utility" (§3 Discussion). Edits: **(a) L48.** OLD: "Exploration value is the value of options not yet sampled. A signal of it cannot name its target, because a named target would be a known option, and choosing a known option is exploitation." Rewrite in the model's terms: exploration value attaches to particular alternatives whose value is uncertain, including ones already tried; what is felt is a cost on the current task; nothing in the feeling displays which alternative carries the value. **(b) L50.** OLD: "On this model the objectlessness belongs to what the signal is about rather than to a failure of introspection. The claim is in-principle on one model only; a theory on which boredom carries implicit information about unconscious goals would weaken it." The model computes the target and the feeling does not display it. So the objectlessness is an occlusion of what is displayed (the epistemic reading), not an absence from the signal's content. Drop "in-principle". Keep the Bench & Lench sentence. **(c) L42 and L100.** "predicts the objectless signal" occurs TWICE: L42 "A computational model with no dualist commitments predicts the objectless signal" and L100 "because a computational model predicts the objectless signal". Change both to "accommodates the objectless signal" (0 words). Locate each by its full clause. **(d) L64.** The exploration reconciliation of the shock data currently contradicts L48. Wilson et al. 2014b (PMC4260463) say "all participants had already experienced the shock", so under L48's definition re-shocking is exploitation. After (a) the reconciliation follows from the model, since a known option of uncertain value has positive VOI. Say so in at most one short clause. Keep "a reconciliation the Map offers and neither party proposes" (verified: neither full text mentions exploration). **(e) L102.** "information about what has not been encountered" becomes "information about alternatives the feeling does not display". **(f) voids/voids.md.** L127 "a computational opportunity-cost model predicts the objectless signal" and L219 "An opportunity-cost model predicts the objectless face": change "predicts" to "accommodates" in both (0 words). Do not attribute to the model anything beyond the quotes above. Append the EXECUTING model to ai_system only if it is ABSENT anywhere in the string. Bump ai_modified to the real `date -u` in each file changed. Changelog, sync, and verify both trees. DRIVER ADDENDUM (2026-11-03, after the pessimistic review): the 2026-11-01 highlight in `obsidian/workflow/highlights.md` (the "### 2026-11-01: Boredom Wants Something Different but Won't Say What" entry, L47) repeats the face-1 claim: "A computational model predicts the objectless signal too". Once the article is fixed, append ONE dated correction line beneath that entry's text (e.g. "*Correction 2026-11-0x: the Agrawal et al. model defines the value of exploration per option; the void is that the feeling does not display its target, not that the signal lacks one.*"), keeping the original text for the record. Do not edit `hugo/static` or any tweet; the posted tweet cannot be changed and must not be deleted.

### ✓ 2026-11-03: Update brain-organoids-and-the-organoid-intelligence-question citations — 2 2020s papers missing (Wood et al. 2025, the published counter to Kosik; the 2026 Asia Pacific consensus), and L47's "not been independently replicated" is outdated by Robbins et al. 2026
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/topics/brain-organoids-and-the-organoid-intelligence-question.md
- **Notes**: From the literature-drift audit of 2026-11-03 (research area `organoid`). This is the article's second audit. The first, on 2026-08-25, found five papers; they were integrated on 08-26. Search (1 call): `brain organoids consciousness sentience 2025 2026 review`.
  **WHICH CHECKS FIRED.** Check 1 (median-year lag) did **not** fire: 17 non-self references, median 2023, 3 years behind against a threshold of 5. Check 2 fired at exactly the threshold: two in-window, topically appropriate papers are uncited (findings 1–2). Check 3: `find_superlative_claims` returned 0, but that only means no pattern matched. One currency claim was found by hand and is outdated (finding 3). Every DOI below is Crossref-verified, and a corpus-wide grep confirms none is cited anywhere on the Map.
  1. **Wood, C., Wang, H., Yang, W.-J., & Xi, Y. (2025). Facing the possibility of consciousness in human brain organoids. *Patterns*, 6(9), 101365. https://doi.org/10.1016/j.patter.2025.101365** — a Perspective; the full text was read from the open-access ORA copy. It is the published counter-statement to Kosik [15], in the same journal. It cites Kosik 2024 and quotes his "fatal flaw". Its summary says: "We argue that this dismissal is premature." On theory-fit, it argues that all current theories except the temporal theory "may be argued to not only accommodate but also to predict the likely development of HBO consciousness." For this it invokes IIT's "islands of awareness" (Bayne, Seth & Massimini 2020), the same phrase Kosik's L65 quotation rejects. It also states "we invert the traditional burden of proof". Its scope is limited to multi-region ("unguided") organoids and assembloids. **Where it goes:** L65. The article gives Kosik's theory-by-theory verdict and then says "The Map takes the anatomy and declines the inference", but that theory-fit reading is itself disputed in print. Saying so supports the Map's thesis that the theories do not settle the question. Wood et al. also contest L63 ("Under that conditional ignorance the facts license no inference in either direction"): they hold that the neural facts *do* raise the probability. Name that disagreement; do not absorb it. **Do not** adopt their "intrinsic active potency" natural-progression argument, and do not read them as support for Reading 1. Their "organoids already playing the computer game Pong" is wrong about DishBrain, which was a planar culture (L47 via Kosik), so do not import it.
  2. **Ishida, S., Kagan, B.J., Kataoka, M., et al., Savulescu, J., & Earp, B.D. (2026). Ethics and regulation of human brain organoid research: recommendations from the Asia Pacific Neuroethics Working Group. *Asian Bioethics Review*, 18(3), 649–679. https://doi.org/10.1007/s41649-025-00398-6** — a 30-author consensus paper from a Singapore meeting in November 2024. The full text was read at Europe PMC (PMC13350604). Its authors include Kagan [3], Lavazza [5], Sawai [7], Niikawa [12], and Holm and Lewis [17]. Verbatim: "potential means of detecting various forms of consciousness (including sentience) in HBOs are not well-established"; "competing theories of consciousness such as Integrated Information Theory and Global Neuronal Workspace Theory have completely divergent implications for the prospect of phenomenal consciousness (or its signs or correlates) in HBOs"; "While current HBOs are very likely to lack the complexity required for sentience (International Society for Stem Cell Research), their potential to develop it raises questions about their treatment." It endorses a precautionary principle over "sentience candidates", moderated by proportionality and citing Birch, and it lists "ethical red herrings" that come from hype. **Where it goes:** the ethics section, L71–L77. Most of the authors that section cites one by one now converge in one consensus, and it corroborates L77 ("there is no accepted, organoid-applicable consciousness-detection procedure"). Its IIT/GNWT sentence is a third published reading of what the theories imply, alongside Kosik's and Wood's. A one-clause cross-reference at L65 could carry both. Corroborating the detection gap is not evidence for the Map's interface framing.
  3. **Currency defect at L47**: "The closed-loop learning effect has not been independently replicated outside the group that reported it". **Robbins, A., Schweiger, H.E., Hernandez, S., et al., Kosik, K.S., Salama, S.R., Sharf, T., Mostajo-Radji, M.A., Haussler, D., & Teodorescu, M. (2026). Goal-directed learning in cortical organoids. *Cell Reports*, 45(2), 116984. https://doi.org/10.1016/j.celrep.2026.116984** — the preprint is bioRxiv 10.1101/2024.12.07.627350. The abstract was read verbatim at Europe PMC (PMID 41720084); the full text was NOT read, so stay at abstract level. This is a UCSC group, independent of Cortical Labs, and **Kosik [15], the article's "independent reading", is a co-author**. In the abstract, *mouse* cortical organoids were embodied in a closed-loop cartpole task. "For most organoids, training signals chosen by artificial reinforcement learning yield better performance than randomly chosen training signals or no training signal, yet improvements do not persist after the 45-min rest period", and blocking AMPA/NMDA receptors abolished the improvement. It is **not** a replication of DishBrain's Pong: the task, the species and the design of the training signal all differ. The sentence can stay only if scoped to the Pong result itself. The paragraph's broader suggestion that closed-loop competence rests on one group no longer holds, and the result was obtained in 3D organoids, which answers Kosik's planar-culture point ("notes the result came from a planar culture rather than an organoid"). **Fix:** scope L47 to the Pong result, and add one clause on Robbins with its limits (mouse tissue; the gain did not persist). "Nothing below is staked on how that dispute resolves" stays true, because all three readings predict this result as well. Check whether the "If the competence turns out to be weaker than advertised" sentence still reads correctly afterwards.
  4. **Optional, only if the budget allows. Kim, J.-i., Imaizumi, K., Jurjuț, O., et al., Scherrer, G., & Pașca, S.P. (2025). Human assembloid model of the ascending neural sensory pathway. *Nature*, 642(8066), 143–153. https://doi.org/10.1038/s41586-025-08808-3** — the abstract was read at Europe PMC (PMC12137141). It describes a four-part human assembloid that joins somatosensory, spinal, thalamic and cortical organoids; "Following noxious chemical stimulation, calcium imaging of hASA demonstrated a coordinated response." Finding 2's consensus cites it to argue that sentience considerations are "increasingly important, not just mere speculation". It bears on L53 ("no body to act with, no world the activity is *about*") and on a picture of the dish that is still pinned to the 2013/2019 single-region organoids. Signalling in a nociceptive pathway is not pain. The result weakens the *no-sensory-input* premise of the disembodiment argument, not the *no-world* premise.
  **Not tasked (lead only):** Balci et al. 2023, "A response to claims of emergent intelligence and sentience in a dish", *Neuron* 111(5), 604–605, doi:10.1016/j.neuron.2023.02.009, and the reply by Kagan et al. 2023, "Scientific communication and the semantics of sentience", *Neuron* 111(5), 606–607, doi:10.1016/j.neuron.2023.02.008. Their metadata is Crossref-verified but their contents were NOT read. They are the published exchange over DishBrain's "sentience" wording that L41–L43 discusses. Both are outside the 24-month window. Cite them only if you read them.
  **LENGTH (copied from the Headroom field, which does not reach the fork):** 3,902/4,000 with the `>=` gate, so **97 words of net growth at most**. Three new reference entries alone cost about 75 words. Work by replacing text rather than adding to it, and offset the growth by trimming redundant prose. Do not trim calibration qualifiers or underdetermination markers. Priority order is 3 > 1 > 2 > 4; drop 4 first, then shorten 2 to a single clause. Re-measure with `analyze_length` after editing, and if the count is ≥ 4,000, cut until it is not. Append new references at the end as [20], [21] and so on, so the existing body numerals [1]–[19] stay valid. Grep every bracketed numeral afterwards.
  **CALIBRATION:** do NOT inflate the article's evidential calibration because it has more citations — see [evidential-status-discipline](/project/evidential-status-discipline/). The pressure runs in **both** directions this time. Wood et al. push toward Reading 1, while Robbins and Kim push "it does more than we said" toward an experiencer inference. The article's thesis is the decoupling, and every new fact here is one that all three readings predict. Keep the three-readings structure and the article's existing voice.

CANONICAL WORDING to match is `topics/motor-control-quantum-zeno` L115. Read it live first, because its same-day P1 may have edited it. In outline: Hagan et al.'s 10⁻⁵–10⁻⁴ s holds "in microtubules only" and is contested (Reimers et al. 2009; McKemmish et al. 2009). At Stapp's ion-channel site, Tegmark's ~10⁻²⁰ s estimate stands. Stapp holds that the hold works on the decohered mixture and "need not outpace decoherence", and Georgiev disputes this. If observation must outpace decoherence, even Hagan's microtubule scale demands at least 3,000 events per 300 ms window, "a rate no concrete model accounts for". Timing is "the largest empirical obstacle". FOUR FILES. (a) `concepts/interface-friction` L60, two sentences. "Hagan et al. (2002) challenged Tegmark's model assumptions, extending estimated coherence times by eight or more orders of magnitude.": eight orders is right only for microtubules (10⁻¹³ → 10⁻⁵–10⁻⁴ s). Scope it to microtubules and say the revision is contested; point to [decoherence](/concepts/decoherence/) rather than adding references unless the page already carries them. "Discrete observation mechanisms like the quantum Zeno effect require only that individual events outpace decoherence, not that coherence span the full decision window.": "require only" presents as an easing the condition the target calls the burden. Replace it with the either-way framing above in one or two sentences, linking [motor-control-quantum-zeno](/topics/motor-control-quantum-zeno/) or [zeno-dynamics-on-a-decohered-mixture](/concepts/zeno-dynamics-on-a-decohered-mixture/). Keep the paragraph's quantum-biology sentence. Budget +40. (b) `concepts/conscious-vs-unconscious-processing` L247: "The timing works: ... The [quantum Zeno mechanism](/concepts/stapp-quantum-mind/) doesn't require sustained coherence but operates through discrete observations (~100 Hz; Stapp 2007, p. 127), making it compatible with established neural timing." The 2026-11-01 Stapp-rate sweep corrected the number and left the conclusion. At ~100 Hz a 300 ms window holds about 30 events, two orders below the 3,000 the target computes if observations must outpace decoherence. Recast "The timing works:" and "making it compatible with established neural timing" so the paragraph reports timing as the open obstacle the target calls it. For example, whether that rate suffices depends on whether the hold must outpace decoherence, which Stapp denies (see [motor-control-quantum-zeno](/topics/motor-control-quantum-zeno/)). Keep the ~100 Hz figure and its citation. ≤ +10, prefer neutral. (c) `concepts/libet-experiments` L127: "read as a race, Zeno freezing would need observations faster than 10⁻¹³ seconds, which nothing in neural dynamics supplies." 10⁻¹³ s is Tegmark's microtubule figure. The target's 2026-10-27 refine (commit a177eddf41) applies only the ~10⁻²⁰ s figure to Stapp's ion-channel site. Change it to 10⁻²⁰ s at that site (e.g. "faster than 10⁻²⁰ seconds at the ion-channel site Stapp's model uses"), and trim within the same paragraph so the file nets ≤ +2 (3,497 of 3,500; the usable ceiling is 3,499). Keep "The proposal stays open, not vindicated." (d) `concepts/von-neumann-wigner-interpretation` L84: "(b) [motor-control-quantum-zeno](/topics/motor-control-quantum-zeno/), which examines whether competing motor plans in Cisek-style affordance populations might supply quantum candidates with longer-lived effective coherence." The target examines no such thing. THREE PARTS plus one optional. (1) BOURGET. (`grep -c -F` = 1 on 2026-11-03; the P1 also edits that paragraph, so re-grep). VERIFIED in the author's archived text (newdualism.org via Wayback 2016-03-27, "Published in Journal of Consciousness Studies 11 (12)"): Bourget sets decoherence aside, since "improper mixtures can be subject to the Zeno effect". He argues that if the will acts by holding an evenly balanced quantum switch through repeated collapses, then with about 100 ms of relevant conscious activity (Libet's 1999 estimate) and a collapse frequency "under 20 to 50hz" (the rate at which successive conscious events can be told apart), the switch "can be collapsed a maximum of 2 to 5 times". The chance that it fails to land in the intended state "is between 3% and 25%", so "weakness of will should be commonplace even in the context of simple choices". He stresses that the result is "independent of the quantum nature of Stapp's proposal". State the argument fairly in a sentence or two, then engage it. The failure rate is (1/2)ⁿ, so everything turns on the collapse-rate premise. At the ~100 Hz the P1 installs there are about 10 probes in Bourget's 100 ms window and about 30 in a 300 ms window (re-derive: (1/2)² = 25%, (1/2)⁵ ≈ 3.1%, (1/2)¹⁰ ≈ 0.1%). That reply works only if probing events need not each be a separately discriminable conscious moment, which is the premise Bourget's 20–50 Hz figure encodes and which the Map would have to argue for. Say whether the reply survives or whether the objection stands as a further open cost beside timing and the sign problem. Do not declare it answered. Keep it distinct from the Georgiev exchange the P1 rewrites. Apply the direct-refutation discipline. Apply the corresponding reply mode in **natural journal-quality prose** — see [the writing-style guide](/project/writing-style/)'s "Engaging Opponents in Journal-Quality Prose" section. **Do not expose mode labels in the article body.** The classification is editor-internal; it belongs in the refine-draft / deep-review changelog entry, not in the article. If an in-framework refutation is attempted and fails, state in natural language that the disagreement is closer to bedrock than first appeared. Reference: Bourget, D. (2004). Quantum leaps in philosophy of mind. *Journal of Consciousness Studies*, 11(12), 17–42. Volume and issue are verified from the author's text. The page range is as carried in `apex/born-preserving-causal-efficacy` ref 3 and `apex/post-decoherence-selection-programme` ref 13; the publisher pages could not be checked, because PhilPapers and Ingenta served bot challenges (do not pass them). Keep L107. Rewrite the L131 clause so the residue is what L107 leaves open: computational accounts explain why effort covaries with difficulty, and what they leave unexplained is why that covariation is felt at all. Then check that the next sentence ("less parsimonious but explanatorily richer") still points at something. ≤ +10. Do not edit L125 (the compatibility P2 owns it). (3) CAI.
