From outer review 2026-11-03 (ChatGPT 5.6 Sol Pro §1, §5; improvements 1–4), adjudicated against the live text. HEADROOM: 3,544 of 4,000 (gate `>=`, 455 usable), shared; budget +70; re-measure first. RUN AFTER the same-day P1 on this page if it is still open. FOUR PARTS. (1) L52 "The answer has largely arrived from neuroscience itself" overclaims, and the lede (L38) already says "rather than settling the matter". Recalibrate: neuroscience has substantially weakened the challenge, and the RP's interpretation is still contested. Cite Schurger, A., Hu, P., Pak, J., & Roskies, A.L. (2021). What is the readiness potential? *Trends in Cognitive Sciences*, 25(7), 558–570. https://doi.org/10.1016/j.tics.2021.04.001 (PubMed 33931306, verified 2026-11-03). Its abstract argues that recent advances "call for a reassessment" of the RP's relevance to volition, which is short of a settled answer. Optional, also verified (PubMed 40799214): Pech, Caspar, Pacherie, Cleeremans & Maoz (2025), *Neuroscience of Consciousness* niaf023, doi 10.1093/nc/niaf023, report "a stronger Readiness Potential" for rewarded than for arbitrary decisions, so the RP-volition relation is unsettled in both directions. For Maoz et al. 2019, link [libet-experiments](/concepts/libet-experiments/) (a frontmatter concept) instead of re-describing it. (2) L54 "reinterprets the readiness potential as an artifact of averaging neural noise rather than evidence of unconscious decision". Scope this to the averaged waveform: the ramp is what averaging threshold-crossing fluctuations would produce, the fluctuations still help set when movement occurs, and the model addresses arbitrary when-to-move tasks. CORRECTION (Claude leg, 2026-11-03, `reviews/outer-review-2026-11-03-claude-opus-5-5.md` Q1 item 1, VERIFIED): the L54 quotation "ongoing stochastic fluctuations in neural activity that favor the spontaneous emission of a movement at certain times more so than at others" is NOT in Schurger, Sitt & Dehaene 2012. It is verbatim in Schurger's single-author 2018 *eNeuro* paper (PMC5815661 full text), where it summarises "Recent evidence" and cites the 2012 paper. The 2012 full text (archived PMC3479453, ~90k characters) contains none of "ongoing stochastic", "spontaneous emission", "certain times" or "more so than". The 2026-07-26 deep review's "Verbatim confirmed at publisher" was a false ratification. Fix it in this part: replace the quotation with the 2012 abstract's own wording (PubMed 22869750, verified), that when the imperative to move is weak the moment of threshold crossing "is largely determined by spontaneous subthreshold fluctuations in neuronal activity", introduced with e.g. "As Schurger and colleagues put it". This keeps the existing 2012 reference. (Alternative: keep the quotation, attribute it to Schurger (2018), and add Schurger, A. (2018). Specific relationship between the shape of the readiness potential, subjective decision time, and waiting time predicted by an accumulator model with temporally autocorrelated input noise. *eNeuro*, 5(1), ENEURO.0302-17.2018. https://doi.org/10.1523/ENEURO.0302-17.2018, Crossref-verified; this costs ~+30.) Aim for length-neutral. (The reviewer misquotes this line as "rather than a decision process"; work from the live text.) (3) L46 "The standard materialist account ... the feeling of 'deciding' to move is an after-the-fact narrative the brain tells itself" is a strawman. Identity and realisation physicalists hold that conscious intending is a neural state that causes later processing; the real dispute is whether that efficacy is exhaustively physical. Recast it (e.g. as one influential materialist reading of Libet) and add a clause naming the physicalist alternative and what the Map disputes. Apply the direct-refutation discipline. Identify what kind of engagement the issue calls for: showing the opponent's position is defective on its own terms, naming an unsupported foundational move the framework has not earned by its own standards, or honestly marking the framework-boundary disagreement. Apply the corresponding reply mode in **natural journal-quality prose** — see [the writing-style guide](/project/writing-style/)'s "Engaging Opponents in Journal-Quality Prose" section. **Do not expose mode labels in the article body.** The classification is editor-internal; it belongs in the refine-draft / deep-review changelog entry, not in the article. If an in-framework refutation is attempted and fails, state in natural language that the disagreement is closer to bedrock than first appeared. (4) L97 "The 200ms window of conscious awareness before movement is not a brief afterthought ...; it is the agent operating within a noisy system". The section sets the Libet timeline aside via Schurger, then reuses Libet's W-time as the interval in which the agent acts. W-time is a clock-report estimate whose value depends on method; `topics/volitional-control` already cites Gavenas, Schurger & Maoz 2025 ("the early readiness potential does not reflect awareness of motor preparation"). Rephrase so the agent's contribution is not pinned to a fixed 200 ms slot (e.g. "Reported awareness, which Libet's clock method places about 200ms before movement, is then ..."). Do NOT edit L68, L95, L105 or L115 (owned by the P1 and the Müller & Rabbitt sweep). ATTRIBUTION: append only your own model id to ai_system, and only if it is ABSENT anywhere in the existing string. Never copy a model id from this note. Set ai_modified from `date -u`. Write the changelog before syncing, run `uv run python scripts/sync.py`, and verify both trees. Report analyze_length before and after. Do not use "This is not X. It is Y." or "load-bearing". CLAUDE-LEG ADDENDUM (2026-11-03, `reviews/outer-review-2026-11-03-claude-opus-5-5.md` Q1 items 2–3; OPTIONAL, its own budget up to +25 on top of the +70, so the task total is at most +95). For part (4): Schultze-Kraft et al. (2016) put the point of no return for vetoing at about 200 ms before movement. The abstract reads "Cancellation of movements was possible if stop signals occurred earlier than 200 ms before movement onset, thus constituting a point of no return" (*PNAS* 113(4), 1080–1085, doi 10.1073/pnas.1513569112; PubMed 26668390 abstract verified). So the 200 ms slot L97 gives the agent is, on that study, the interval in which movement can no longer be cancelled. `concepts/libet-experiments` already cites the study. Bredikhin, Germanova, Nikulin & Klucharev (2023), "(Non)-experiencing the intention to move: On the comparisons between the Readiness Potential onset and Libet's W-time", *Neuropsychologia* 185, 108570, doi 10.1016/j.neuropsychologia.2023.108570 (Crossref-verified, existence only), bears on W-report validity. Read it before citing any claim from it. For part (1): the reviewer names the strongest pre-awareness evidence on the other side as single-unit data. Fried, Mukamel & Kreiman (2011), *Neuron* 69(3), 548–562, doi 10.1016/j.neuron.2010.11.045 (PubMed 21315264 abstract verified): "A population of 256 SMA neurons is sufficient to predict in single trials the impending decision to move with accuracy greater than 80% already 700 ms prior to subjects' awareness". One clause naming it keeps the recalibrated section two-sided. `concepts/motor-selection` L141 already reads it as threshold-crossing dynamics seen one cell at a time, which you may echo. Gavenas, Rutishauser, Schurger & Maoz (2024), *Nature Communications* 15, 7285, doi 10.1038/s41467-024-51401-x (Crossref, existence only), gives a classical spiking-network account of the slow ramp; optional. GEMINI-LEG ADDENDUM (2026-11-03, `reviews/outer-review-2026-11-03-gemini-3-1-pro.md` Verification Notes; no extra budget): Gemini cited Bogler et al. for the noise-only reading, but the paper argues the reverse. Bogler, C., Grujičić, B., & Haynes, J.-D. (2023). Clarifying the nature of stochastic fluctuations and accumulation processes in spontaneous movements. *Frontiers in Psychology*, 14, 1271180. https://doi.org/10.3389/fpsyg.2023.1271180 (Crossref ✓; the abstract is read through OpenAlex; the 2021 bioRxiv version is doi 10.1101/2021.06.04.447111). Its abstract says the evidence for stochastic fluctuations "is so far only indirect", a model deterministic during accumulation "can explain the data in a similar way", and "the deterministic component of the model is quantitatively the dominant input". It is a published counterweight that supports part (2)'s scoping of L54's "artifact of averaging neural noise". OPTIONAL: cite it only inside this task's existing budget, and prefer it to Pech et al. 2025 if only one counterweight fits. Do not add Gemini's other weakness sources: Georgiev 2021 *Symmetry* has the reverse conclusion, Jiang et al. has wrong metadata and is off topic, and Gibb et al. 2021 is unlocatable.

From outer review 2026-11-03 (ChatGPT 5.6 Sol Pro §3, §6 item 2; improvements 11, 13, 14, 15), adjudicated against the live text at ~04:15Z. HEADROOM: 3,544 of 4,000 (gate `>=`, so 455 words usable); budget +90; re-measure first, since two same-day P2s on this page share the headroom. The same page is the subject of the same-day Claude and Gemini outer reviews; read their review files if present. FOUR PARTS. (1) CORE. L95 (re-grep "selects among salient options by stabilising one through attention") and L105 (re-grep "shapes which state actualises by selecting among existing possibilities") present the Zeno mechanism as the agent choosing which option actualises. The Map's own pages say otherwise. `concepts/motor-selection` L48 says the mechanism "controls which neural state is measured and how often, with outcomes still Born-governed. That is Stapp's Process-1 placement, not the *outcome selection* the Map's Bidirectional Interaction tenet adopts" (see also its L172). `concepts/stapp-quantum-mind` L64 has the agent supply the question and leaves the answer to "nature's choice between 'Yes' and 'No'"; its L106 has attention hold a pattern "once nature has" delivered it. Rewrite L95/L105 to match. On Stapp's account the agent fixes which question is put and how often, and rapid repetition holds a pattern once nature's Born-distributed answer has delivered it. Outcome selection, which the tenet adopts, needs a further mechanism. Point to [process-1-specification-problem](/concepts/process-1-specification-problem/) (already linked at L115) or [motor-selection](/concepts/motor-selection/). In the same paragraph, L95 "The neuroscience reviewed here positions the agent at a specific juncture" conflicts with L99's own concession that a classical accumulator is causally closed. Rephrase it as identifying the juncture where an agent would have to act, length-neutral. Leave L99 intact and do not imply the Map adopts the Zeno route. (2) GEORGIEV. L115 "Georgiev (2015) replies that a mind projecting in the basis decoherence has already fixed is redundant with it" reports one horn only. The arXiv 1412.4741 abstract (verified 2026-11-03) says the simulations "unambiguously showed that the quantum Zeno effect breaks down for timescales greater than the brain decoherence time", proves that "local projections cannot decrease the von Neumann entropy of the unconditional brain density matrix", and leaves open "a decoherence-free subspace". `concepts/process-1-specification-problem` L57–69 states the dilemma: in a different basis the effect breaks down; in the same basis (Stapp's 2012 concession) the charge is redundancy. State both horns in about one sentence, matching that page. Keep the existing engagement (report Stapp and Georgiev, take no side). (3) STAPP'S RATE. After "at least 3,000 discrete observation events, and 30,000 at the 10⁻⁵-second end", transfer the comparison the 2026-11-01 deep review installed on `concepts/quantum-zeno-effect`: "The model Stapp describes runs two orders below even the lower figure: 10 milliseconds between probing actions, about 100 per second (Stapp 2007, p. 127), or some thirty per window". Stapp 2007 is already reference 17. Adapt the wording; do not invent figures. Keep "a rate no concrete model accounts for" consistent with it. (4) TEGMARK. Tegmark is discussed at L115 but absent from References. Add: Tegmark, M. (2000). Importance of quantum decoherence in brain processes. *Physical Review E*, 61(4), 4194–4206. https://doi.org/10.1103/PhysRevE.61.4194 (Crossref-verified 2026-11-03). Then fix "Tegmark's original calculations placed neural coherence times at femtoseconds—a [twelve-order-of-magnitude gap](/concepts/timing-gap-problem/) from the millisecond timescales of neural competition". Tegmark's figures are 10⁻¹³ s (microtubules) and 10⁻²⁰ s (ion/neuron firing). Twelve orders holds only from 10⁻¹³ s to a ~300 ms decision window, which is how `concepts/timing-gap-problem` L37/L63 computes it; the 2026-10-27 changelog recorded this phrase as "not checked". For example: "placed neural coherence times at 10⁻¹³ to 10⁻²⁰ seconds, twelve or more orders of magnitude below a ~300-millisecond decision window", keeping the [...](/concepts/timing-gap-problem/) pipe. Optional: "stands unrevised" → "has no mechanism-specific revision the Map has found". REJECTED, do not act: "Hagan used as a generic bound" (L115 already says "in microtubules only"), and "acceleration is more common is too broad" (Kofman & Kurizki 2000, "appears to be much more ubiquitous", is quoted on the linked `sign-problem-for-conscious-observation` L45). Do NOT edit L68 (owned by the Müller & Rabbitt sweep P2) or the L38/L81/L83/L89/L119 sentences (owned by the compatibility-as-location P2). ATTRIBUTION: append only your own model id to ai_system, and only if it is ABSENT anywhere in the existing string (currently `claude-opus-4-6+claude-opus-5+claude-fable-5+claude-fable-5-1`). Never copy a model id from this note. Set ai_modified from `date -u`. Write the changelog before syncing, run `uv run python scripts/sync.py`, and verify both trees. Report analyze_length before and after. Do not use "This is not X. It is Y." or "load-bearing". CLAUDE-LEG ADDENDUM (2026-11-03 ~04:55Z, `reviews/outer-review-2026-11-03-claude-opus-5-5.md` Q4 items 1–2; this part carries its own +15 on top of the +90, so the task total is +105). (5) FIREWALL. L109 says the case "would survive, needing a different mechanism to fill the same role" (re-grep that sentence; `grep -c -F` = 1 on 2026-11-03), and L99 calls the Zeno proposal "(or a successor mechanism that supplies real indeterminacy)" the source of the agency argument's opening. Neither names the successor. The register does: [P-Q1](/positions/quantum-interface/#p-q1) ranks [post-decoherence-selection](/concepts/post-decoherence-selection/) as the strongest current mechanism candidate, and [P-Q4](/positions/quantum-interface/#p-q4) demotes Stapp's Zeno mechanism below it. `concepts/motor-selection` L176 already says so, and adds that post-decoherence selection is an outcome-selection proposal, so the Zeno probing story is not inherited. When you rewrite L95/L105 under part (1), name that successor in L109 in about one clause (bare position IDs [P-Q1](/positions/quantum-interface/#p-q1)/[P-Q4](/positions/quantum-interface/#p-q4) autolink), so that "would survive" points at a named, register-ranked candidate rather than at future physics. Optional, Crossref-verified 2026-11-03: Stapp, H. (2015). Reply to Georgiev: No-go for Georgiev's no-go theorem. *NeuroQuantology*, 13(2). https://doi.org/10.14704/nq.2015.13.2.851. Add it only if part (2)'s sentence reports Stapp's response, and read it first. REJECTED from the Claude leg, do not act: (a) "supply an order-of-magnitude Zeno-time / bath-correlation estimate". L115 already states the operative condition in Kiefer & Joos's terms and says no source computes where a brain sits; an estimate would be the Map inventing physics. (b) "Kiefer & Joos is cited for a stronger claim than it makes". The condition is the conjunction of two K&J statements, both verified in arXiv quant-ph/9803052 and quoted on `concepts/zeno-dynamics-on-a-decohered-mixture` L51/L53. (c) "Stapp 2000 puts the locus in the low-frequency EM field, against SSB 2005's ion channels". Stapp 2000 introduces it with "Suppose, for definiteness", as an illustration. COMBINE ADD-ON (2026-11-03 synthesis, inside part (5)'s +15, no extra words): ChatGPT §6 item 4 and improvement 27 warn that post-decoherence selection "changes the theory rather than repairing Zeno stabilization" and should be treated "as a rival interface proposal with different predictions and liabilities, not as a repair that can be silently substituted when decoherence defeats Zeno control". Claude asks for the successor to be named; ChatGPT asks that it not be slipped in as a fix. One clause can meet both. Name post-decoherence selection in L109 as the register-ranked rival ([P-Q1](/positions/quantum-interface/#p-q1), [P-Q4](/positions/quantum-interface/#p-q4)), and say in the same clause that it is an outcome-selection proposal that does not inherit the Zeno probing story, as `concepts/motor-selection` L176 puts it. Do not present it as a drop-in for the Zeno hold. The article has 0 hits for "post-decoherence" today, so ChatGPT's "uses it more like a protective fallback" describes the unnamed L99/L109 successor, not a named one. Separately, ChatGPT and Claude disagree on L115's "twelve-order" (ChatGPT: unstable when measured from milliseconds; Claude: right at the slow end, 10⁻¹³ against 10⁻¹ s). Part (4)'s suggested wording, anchored to a ~300 ms window, satisfies both; keep it.

### ✓ 2026-11-03: `concepts/psychophysical-social-choice-problem`: add the question-selection (Process 1) form of the composition problem, and a +0 link to scale-types at the cross-subject unit
- **Type**: refine-draft

- **Notes**: NOT COVERED anywhere: process-1-specification-problem, quantum-zeno-effect, motor-control-quantum-zeno, stapp-quantum-mind, multi-agent-born-preservation-problem and multi-mind-collapse-problem have 0 hits for a multi-subject Process 1 question (grep 2026-10-19). HEADROOM: 2,937 of 3,500 (concepts hard; gate `>=`; usable ceiling 3,499, so 562 words). Target +100–140. FENCE: do NOT edit the paragraph beginning "The result depends on which Born-preservation reading is in force" (§born-favours-fixed-weights, ~L80). The open P1 HUMAN DECISION on [P-Q2](/positions/quantum-interface/#p-q2)'s quantifier holds it. Do not edit any positions file. (1) In §composition-rule, after "Selection models are built differently, and they inherit the problem wherever two subjects' selections bear on one event." (~L46), add a short paragraph on a third family. On Stapp's Process 1 route, which the Bidirectional Interaction tenet registers without adopting, consciousness fixes *what is asked* (projector and timing), and nature's answer stays Born-distributed. Link [zeno-dynamics-on-a-decohered-mixture](/concepts/zeno-dynamics-on-a-decohered-mixture/#unconditional) ("the mind contributes the asking: which question, and when") and [process-1-specification-problem](/concepts/process-1-specification-problem/) (the Laskey variant: brain-supplied basis, mind-supplied timing). This is MAP'S WORKING and must be labelled so. Re-derive the arithmetic yourself before writing (a non-selective projection along Bloch axis n maps r → (r·n)n): (a) Two subjects asking incompatible questions of shared tissue get order-dependent results, and rapid alternation shrinks the state by cos²θ per round, so each undoes the other's hold. (b) On the Laskey variant, concordant subjects pose the same question and their schedules merge. Inside the quadratic window the hold then strengthens: the Zeno page's toy R/Γ = 1 − (1 − e⁻ˣ)/x goes 0.136 → 0.071 when τ halves at x = 0.3, and almost nothing changes outside the window. (c) The fixed-weights result does not carry over, because a Zeno hold is designed to move the unconditional statistics, and a joint schedule composes channels rather than mixing conditionals. You may refer to §born-favours-fixed-weights for the contrast (fixed mixtures renormalise concordant subjects) WITHOUT editing it. Keep the scope to the boundary cases locality leaves (§locality). Do not imply the Map adopts the Zeno route, which it ranks behind post-decoherence selection. (2) +0 piped link: in §intensity, put [...](/concepts/scale-types-for-phenomenal-quantities/) on the existing words "share a unit across subjects". That page's §The Cross-Subject Rung shows that within-subject axioms never deliver interpersonal comparability. If you mention the invariance formalisation, note that the SEP (List 2022, already ref 6) credits Sen (1970b) for it, NOT Sen 1977. Do not attribute it to Sen 1977, and do not add a reference. (3) OPTIONAL SECONDARY, +0 ONLY, REPORT each as done or skipped: `obsidian/concepts/scale-types-for-phenomenal-quantities.md` is 3,492 of 3,500, so at most a +0 piped link on "interpersonal utility comparison" to [psychophysical-social-choice-problem](/concepts/psychophysical-social-choice-problem/). In `obsidian/concepts/zeno-dynamics-on-a-decohered-mixture.md` (2,630), at most a +0 piped link from its Relation to Site Perspective "which question, and when" to this page. The new page grades dissociation inference in three tiers: separate failure is robust; functional differentiation is abductive and earned only against rivals actually modelled; discrete components or "sub-systems"/channels need contested bridge assumptions (transparency, locality, subtractivity). It also holds that a cross-literature pairing of two syndromes measured on different scales falls short of the formal double-dissociation criterion (its L42 on the blindsight-neglect pairing). Each passage below states a tier-three or cross-literature claim at a higher grade. (1) voids/recognition-void L118 (Relation to Site Perspective, Dualism): "The Capgras-prosopagnosia double dissociation establishes that recognition fractionates into separately damageable sub-systems". "establishes ... sub-systems" is tier three; tier two would be e.g. "is best explained by" functionally distinct contributions. The phrase now carries a zero-word link to the page, so keep the linked words or re-pipe them. (2) topics/capgras-delusion-and-the-affective-recognition-channel L37: "the mirror-image pairing that establishes recognition as a compound of at least two components", same grade issue. (3) concepts/blindsight L68: "This double dissociation (blindsight: processing without experience; lucid dreaming: experience without input) supports the view that phenomenal rendering is a distinct capability". This pairs two conditions from different literatures and measures, so it is at most a cross-literature contrast; keep the zero-word link on "double dissociation". (4) topics/anarchic-hand-and-action-ownership L88: "agency and ownership *doubly dissociate*, which shows authorship is an interface signal rather than a given". "shows ... interface signal" states the interface reading as an inference from the dissociation, while the page's own description says "interface, not proof". L66/L68 ("a genuine double dissociation", "constructed signal") may stand at tier two. Do not touch the hub (topics/neurological-dissociations-as-interface-architecture), which has its own P3. ### ✓ 2026-11-02: `topics/the-consequence-argument-for-incompatibilism` — say that the argument also reaches the mentally-determined dualist installed at L91, and that the Mind argument is its formal twin (Finch 2017 reverses the Finch & Warfield 1998 asymmetry the page already cites) (optimistic-2026-10-19 incompatibilism repair wing, High 1; +240 vs 1,315 headroom)
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/topics/the-consequence-argument-for-incompatibilism.md
- **Notes**: HEADROOM 2,684/4,000 (1,315) by `analyze_length` at 11:05Z. The suggested wording below measured +240 on a mirrored scratch copy (→ 2,924). That is under the 3,000 soft threshold; keep it under, and trim before adding anything. TWO INSERTIONS + ONE REFERENCE; each OLD string was counted once at 11:05Z. (1) L91: after `which a dualist who held conscious contributions to be fixed by prior mental states could also accept.` insert: `That dualist would not escape the Consequence Argument: on a dualist picture its "state of the world" and "laws of nature" take in mental states and psychophysical laws, so if each selection were fixed by prior mental states, the transfer of powerlessness would run as before. Quantum openness gives a conscious contribution room; only a selection that prior mental states do not fix either discharges the libertarian premise, which is why that discharge is agent-causal rather than merely quantum.` Grounding: the page's own L35 definition of determinism quantifies over "the state of the world" and "the laws of nature". `topics/free-will` L80 and `concepts/agent-causation` L59 already make agent causation irreducible to prior *mental* events, so this states a consequence of existing commitments, not a new one. (2) L95: after `They are distinct problems, and the Map answers them at different points—though the luck objection may be the harder of the two, and clearing the compatibilist field does nothing to answer it.` insert: `They are also formally twinned. Van Inwagen (1983) held the Consequence Argument valid if and only if the Mind argument is valid, the formal luck argument that free will is incompatible with indeterminism. Finch and Warfield (1998) read the McKay–Johnson result as favouring libertarians, since only the Consequence Argument seemed repairable; Finch (2017) has since argued that a formulation of the Mind argument is valid if and only if the Consequence Argument is. Libertarians must then give up the Consequence Argument, find a false premise in the Mind argument, or hold with van Inwagen that one of its plausible premises is false without knowing which. The Map's agent-causal settler bids for the second; its admission that the settler's modal structure is owed rather than supplied ([[free-will]]) keeps it near the third.` (3) References: add before the Finch & Warfield entry: `1. Finch, A. (2017). Revisiting the Mind Argument. In J. A. Keller (Ed.), *Being, Freedom, and Method: Themes from the Philosophy of Peter van Inwagen* (pp. 118–139). Oxford University Press. https://doi.org/10.1093/acprof:oso/9780198715702.003.0007` (Crossref-verified 2026-10-19: pages, editor, DOI). SOURCES VERIFIED 2026-10-19 against OpenAlex abstracts. Finch 2017: "In An Essay on Free Will, van Inwagen argued that the consequence argument is valid if and only if the Mind argument is"; "This paper argues that Finch and Warfield were wrong: there is a formulation of the Mind argument that is valid if and only if the consequence argument is"; "libertarians must jettison the consequence argument, find a flaw in the premises of the Mind argument, or join van Inwagen in holding that although each of those premises seems true, at least one is false." Finch & Warfield 1998: "We show how to repair the Consequence argument and argue that no similar improvement will revive the worry about the compatibility of indeterminism and freedom." CAUTIONS: (a) the "valid iff" claim about van Inwagen 1983 comes through Finch 2017's abstract. Either attribute it that way ("as Finch 2017 reports") or locate it in the *Essay* before citing 1983 bare. (b) Do NOT name which Mind-argument premise the agent-causalist denies unless you verify it at source; the generic "find a false premise" wording is deliberate. (c) "a primitive whose modal structure is owed rather than supplied" is the wording of `topics/free-will` L94. Check that it is still there before echoing it. (d) The 10-15 deep review's stability notes fence L50 (agglomeration), L27/L68 (origin) and L97 (Lewis lawfulness), and this task touches none of them. The L95 luck-objection calibration that the 07-19 review praised is kept and extended, not rewritten. (e) The edit makes the Map MORE exposed, because the luck objection becomes the formal price of the Consequence Argument, so it cannot be an over-claim regression. (f) Do not use "This is not X. It is Y." or "load-bearing".

### ✓ 2026-11-02: `concepts/phenomenal-concepts-strategy` L75 — Chalmers's master-argument horn 2 omits premise (5) ("zombies do not share our epistemic situation") and gives zombies "the same apparent gap", which is the Carruthers–Veillet reply, not Chalmers's premise
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/concepts/phenomenal-concepts-strategy.md
- **Notes**: Hub L75 currently reads: "Zombies would have the same concepts, the same apparent gap. So C doesn't explain why *we* find consciousness puzzling—the zombie finds it equally puzzling for purely physical reasons." Chalmers 2007 §3.2 (author PDF consc.net/papers/pceg.pdf, verified 2026-11-02 by text extraction) runs: (4) if P&~C is not conceivable, zombies satisfy C; (5) zombies do NOT share our epistemic situation, defined as "the truth-values of their beliefs and the epistemic status of their beliefs"; (6) so C cannot explain our epistemic situation. He says "the real work in this argument is done by premise (5)", and its intuitive ground is that a zombie's corresponding beliefs are false "or at least ... less justified". As written, L75 drops (5), and "the same apparent gap" makes the step to "C doesn't explain" a non sequitur. It also undercuts the hub's own L97, where Carruthers & Veillet's reply is precisely that zombie and twin DO share the epistemic situation. Model wording, now live at `arguments/functionalism-argument` L165: "zombies have C too, yet their beliefs about consciousness are false or less justified, so C cannot explain our epistemic situation." A correct sibling is `topics/modal-structure-of-phenomenal-properties` L95 ("yet the zombie doesn't face a genuine hard problem"). SECONDARY loci, same family, in the same pass if cheap: `research/phenomenal-concepts-strategy-2026-01-14.md` L87, the origin, which says "zombies would have the same epistemic situation as us", the opposite of premise (5); and `archive/topics/phenomenal-concepts-as-materialist-response.md` L82. Separately, `concepts/knowledge-argument` L84 renders the dilemma as "so zombies could have them, contradicting the claim that acquiring them needs experience". That is a different reading not checked in this pass: verify it against §3 before touching it. Do not change the hub's L79 Carruthers–Veillet framing, which is correct.

So something that guides action crossed. Recast the lede as a graded claim: prior experience carries a durable but attenuated trace that guides action, and what fails to cross is the full motivational force the state has while it is occurring. Recast the "Content Crosses, Weight Does Not" framing only as far as the lede change requires. The reviewer's dilemma, which the article should answer: if "weight" means any lasting effect on later choice, some weight crossed; if it means the full state, the claim reduces to the trivial point that remembering pain is not being in pain. Say in one sentence which tier the evidence reaches. The tiers are non-reinstantiation, attenuation, forecasting error and structural unrepresentability. The reviewer grants the first three and denies the fourth. (2) IMMUNISING MOVE at L65: "on this article's reading, it works by installing a partial state, not by improving a description ... none improves the cold representation as a representation". On simulationist accounts, partial activation is one way representation works. As written, no cold-side success could count against the thesis. Either give an independent criterion that separates a representation whose influence grows from partial state induction, plus a prospective falsifier, or downgrade the sentence to an interpretation the data do not decide. Use Ozdemir et al. 2024 as the test case: Ozdemir, S., Gonzalez, J. M., Bansal, P., Huynh, V. A., Sng, B. L., & Finkelstein, E., "Getting it right with discrete choice experiments: Are we hot or cold?", *Social Science & Medicine* 348, 116850, https://doi.org/10.1016/j.socscimed.2024.116850. The driver verified the PubMed abstract (PMID 38608481): 248 individuals; survey "administered upon hospital admission for childbirth"; DCE choice concordance with actual epidural use "71-60%", "77-76%" when accounting for initial use of other ineffective modalities; direct elicitation 58%. A structured cold-side elicitation improved prediction of hot-state behaviour without inducing the state. The article may classify it as inferential charting, which L65/L75 already allow, but must then say so explicitly. Do not call it a counterexample, or deny that it is one, without the criterion. Adding it is optional if the length budget forbids; making L65 non-immunising is required. (3) STRUCTURAL OVERREACH: L39 "It survives disclosure" and L57 "This one does not ... because there is nothing to restore it from" rest on a single corrective instruction in one fatigue-attribution study (Nordgren et al. 2006, quoted accurately). Narrow both to what that study shows. Make explicit the three-way separation that L75's "correction factor without restored access" already implies: recreating the state, estimating it intuitively, and correcting behaviour for a known bias. PARTIAL FALSE ALARM: the reviewer says the article "slides among all three", but L75 already draws the access/correction line. Make it explicit in a clause; do not add a section. L97 (Occam) says the cold self's explanation is "systematically wrong and uncorrectable by instruction", which generalises from the same single study; scope it (e.g. "in the fatigue study"). (4) If words allow, L81 "helps less than it should" lacks a benchmark (reviewer §9.5). Optional. GUARDS, keep all of these: the 2026-10-26 02:00 refine installs (changelog entry, "Reported, not edited" list included), namely "closed to the cold side but open to re-entry", "survives disclosure" (narrow it, never revert to "conceals itself"), Morley "One can retrieve the episode, and often its quality, without re-experiencing it", the Hume "this article's reading, not Hume's" fence, the tenet section's "compatible with the data rather than supported by them", the epiphenomenalist concession, and the Noordhof reversal quote. Read `reviews/pessimistic-2026-10-11-cross-state-void.md` first (Issues 1–4 installed the current calibration). COORDINATION: the citation-currency items (Fisher & Rangel 2014 for the hot-to-cold direction, the Helton & Register abstract-only disclaimer, Scarry, L75 Ulysses, L89) belong to the sibling P2 minted the same day. Leave them to it unless your cuts free the words. Apply the direct-refutation discipline: name the unsupported move (redescribing counterevidence as state entry) in natural journal-quality prose, per the writing-style guide's "Engaging Opponents in Journal-Quality Prose"; do not expose mode labels in the article body. Set ai_modified from `date -u`. Append the running model (grep your own task output) to ai_system only if it is ABSENT anywhere in `claude-fable-5-1+claude-opus-5-5`. Re-measure with analyze_length after editing. Changelog before sync; verify the obsidian and hugo trees; no commit. CLAUDE ADD-ON (2026-11-02, from claude-opus-5-5; driver-verified): (a) ITEM (1) HAS A SECOND SOURCE. Ref 8 (Nordgren, McDonnell & Loewenstein 2011), Study 3, full text: "Experiencing cold temperature just 10 min prior to answering the pain and ethicality questions did not affect participants' evaluations". The prior-cold group rated the tactic less painful (M = 5.48) than the cold group (M = 6.41). So a pain-severity estimate, the lede's "roughly how strong", failed to cross after ten minutes. The graded lede should not claim that intensity crosses. The L43 figures are CONFIRMED in the full text (Exhibit 7, WTAP1: sensation/delayed 3.9, distraction/delayed 5.6, control 9.9; scale 0-15), so item (1) stands on them. (b) ITEM (2): the reviewer offers a falsifier usable as-is. A trained forecaster whose cold-state willingness-to-accept matches their hot-state willingness-to-accept, with no change in current interoceptive markers, would count as a cold-side representational improvement. (c) L65 "Induction closes it": the article reports that the induced teachers' recommendations shifted, not that their estimates became accurate. Use "shifts" or "narrows". (d) L53 "this held for one's own past behaviour as much as for another's": the abstract (PubMed 16866751) says only that the gap "was observed when people made attributions about the past behavior of another person or themselves". Change "as much as" to "as well as" (0 words). (e) L57 "Reading about one's own 2 a.m. binge from the sated side produces bafflement" states an illustration as a finding. Mark it as illustrative ("can produce"). All of (c) to (e) are length-neutral. GEMINI ADD-ON (2026-11-02, from gemini-3-1-pro; driver-verified): (a) The same single-study generalisation sits in the description, L3 "and knowing the bias does not remove it". Gemini read it (via the `voids/voids.md` L306 entry, which the taxonomy P2 owns) as the article's headline claim. Narrow L3 to match item (3). The description is frontmatter, outside analyze_length, so this costs no body words. The same-day neutrality P2 also edits L3 ("felt weight"); if you run first, make both changes in one pass and leave the field consistent for whichever runs second. (b) DO NOT CITE Gemini's evidence for corrigibility. "A major 2025 meta-analysis by Kim de Jong and colleagues" (APA *Monitor*, January 2025), said to show the gap "vanished entirely" under episodic future thinking, was not found: OpenAlex's Kim de Jong (Leiden) publishes on psychotherapy progress feedback, and no empathy-gap meta-analysis turned up. "Yang et al., 2025" has no source. Treat both as fabricated or misattributed. Steinmetz et al. 2018 (L65) stays the article's test case for cold-side simulation. COMBINE ADD-ON (2026-11-02, synthesis): (a) L93 restates the lede's split ("The descriptive content crosses and changes little; the weight fails to cross and its absence changes choices measurably"). When item (1) makes the lede graded, bring that sentence into line in the same pass, length-neutral. Keep the paragraph's "compatible with the data rather than supported by them" and the epiphenomenalist concession. (b) The two verified reviewers attack the content/weight split from opposite flanks. ChatGPT shows that action-guiding weight partly crosses (Read & Loewenstein, a week later). Claude shows that the intensity estimate does not cross intact (ref 8, Study 3). The graded lede must claim neither a clean description nor a total failure of weight. (c) UNVERIFIED lead for item (2): Claude says Steinmetz et al. conclude that "simulation affects current feelings, which (at least partly) explains" the preference shifts. Neither processing pass checked this. If it is verbatim at the source, the partial-state reading has the authors' support, but item (2) still requires the criterion. The defect is that L65 treats the reading as defeating the objection; whether the reading has support is a separate question.
